This section replaces itself as code lands, as LLP 1000's did; the journal
carries the chronology. After week four, 2026-09-12 (0.1.0):

| `benchmarks/runs/2026-09-12-snapback4-clique-0002/`, `benchmarks/runs/2026-09-12-snapback4-loopbar/` | the Clique hub run on 0.0.2 (Astra medium, media and scheduled work struck): every milestone green in 7,725 s, graded 69/80 applicable with two majors read against main, M7 green in 917 s; the loop bar met on 0.0.11 (3/3, 443–537 s, no attributed loop); every finding and its fix | the run's landings |

| `crates/snapback4-core/src/assets.rs`, `engine/job.rs`, `crates/snapback4/src/assets.rs` | 0.1.0, the whole brief: `expire at .col` (absent everywhere at once, swept, streamed); assets (sniffed JPEG/PNG/WebP/MP4, one asset one home, served by the home row's read rule, `once`); jobs (`chunk` sweeps, a page per commit with its cursor, parked on refusal) and `schedule … every N [at HH:MM]` (once per boundary across restarts); the maintenance thread; the client's `upload`/`asset`/`assetSource`/`useAsset` | the 0.1.0 landing |

| `benchmarks/runs/2026-09-13-snapback4-clique-0003/` | the Clique hub run on 0.1.0, the whole brief, nothing struck: every milestone green in 7,002 s (M8 at 6,926 s), graded 84/1/16 of 101 (O1 62, O2 48, O4 35, O5 band 30), M7 capability-failure on the app's pre-existing logout crash after delivering its polls and migration; eight fix-forward releases the same night (0.1.1–0.1.8: one thread per request, asset facts on the argument, `a if cond else b`, `sum(page, .x)`, a one-second clock, the paged snapshot with `N_NO_HORIZON`, the outbox in submission order, `N_WIDE_MERGE`); then what the run said to do next: the `feeds` card (fan-out on write, compiled and run in `tests/clique.rs`), `onSession`/`useSession` for the sign-out contract the app's logout crash exposed, and the simulator witness proving a cold start offline and writes composed offline surviving a restart on SQLite | the run's landings, 0.1.12 |

| `crates/snapback4-device` | one device over a `DeviceStore` (`store.rs`): `device.rs` applies the stream with the watermark, stages paged snapshots, keeps the outbox in submission order over confirmed images, answers queries and predictions through `engine::local`; `sqlite.rs` binds the core's store on a native host with the C ABI (`ffi.rs`, `include/snapback4.h`); `memory.rs` is the store for the web, transactions as an undo log, each commit's changes reported for the host to persist; `call.rs` is the one JSON dispatcher; `web.rs` the wasm surface (open with what the host kept, call, drain). `tests/device.rs` runs every scenario on both stores against a real engine; `tests/native.rs` what only SQLite has (two devices on one store, a failing store). Measured whole as wasm: 312,924 B gzip before the diet | the native landing; the one-implementation landing |

| `packages/snapback4-expo`, `apps/chat-expo/src/client.ts`, `witness/expo.mjs` (`WITNESS_DEV_CLIENT=1`) | the Expo module: Swift over the C ABI, the Rust library as a 9.1 MB xcframework (staticlib alone, stripped; 3.3 MB on npm), `nativeAvailable()` choosing the tier at import; the simulator witness on a development build passes the five proofs on the module (303 rows synced in 31 ms, reopen 6 ms, cold start offline 2 ms) → `metrics/witness.jsonl` `dev-client-native`; `snapback4-expo@0.1.14` on npm | the Expo module landing |

| `crates/snapback4-core/src/engine/native.rs`, `crates/snapback4-native`, `crates/snapback4/src/native.rs`, `native-fixtures/rescore`, `crates/snapback4-sim/tests/native.rs` | native jobs (LLP 3000.005): the wasm host over the chunk's `Runner` (get, lookup, scan, mint, insert, insert-if-absent, update, upsert, delete, log, now), one chunk per call with the module's state in the job row, fuel per chunk, refusals and traps parking the job; the guest SDK (`Ctx`, `Chunk`, `Next`, `jobs!`, `from_row`/`row_of`); `check` writing `native.rs` and building `native.wasm`, `dev` rebuilding on save, `deploy` shipping the module, `doctor` and `check --cost` reporting it; the fixture: Interview's rescore in Rust with a float exponent, resumed across three chunks, plus a write past the ceiling and a panic, each parked with its reason | the native-jobs landing |

| `crates/snapback4-core/src/words.rs`, `engine/read.rs` (`scan_words`), `engine/effects.rs`, `identity.rs` (services, custom sessions), `engine/mod.rs` (`Presence`), `crates/snapback4/src/serve.rs` (`/effects/*`, `/auth/session`, `/auth/revoke`, the `Live` registry), `verbs.rs` (`token`), `packages/snapback4/src/worker.ts` | 0.2.1, what Clique and Interview still needed: word-prefix search on one index shape on every host; effects claimed and settled by a worker with a service token; service seats and sessions for the application's own accounts; `connected[p]`; the feed ratio measured at 1× and the ranked recipe on the `feeds` card | the 2026-09-13 landing |

Not in this slice by decision: relevance-ranked search, an identity
provider in the engine, push delivery itself (`NOT-DOING.md`).

## 9. Architecture and code review, 2026-09-12

The author's request for a comprehensive review adds a findings record to
this root before opening focused research children. The review covers the
engine, compiler, HTTP/CLI host, both client tiers and stores, React arms,
reference apps, simulator, fixtures, witnesses and release tooling at
Snapback 4 tree `cf0a13dca32b3e1721d1d947a3f82c262a0807f0`
(repository commit `d6c803878a5432cce5f045777786a049972d3d70`, version
0.0.16). It does not change the bakeoff decisions or accept new designs.

The prescribed suite passes, but additional probes reproduce
schema-admission and projection failures, lost or regressed replica state,
incorrect predictions, stale observations, compiler semantic errors and
request-worker starvation. Passing the existing suite does not establish
the missing properties. The author's updated scope reserves security holes
for another model; this record covers architecture, correctness,
performance and ergonomics and makes no security-review claim.

Four questions need design work beyond an isolated patch:

- [LLP 3000.000](./3000.000-atomic-schema-and-release-activation.research.md):
  the atomic unit and owner of schema/release activation.
- [LLP 3000.001](./3000.001-durable-replica-and-write-lifecycle.research.md):
  confirmed replica data, speculative writes and durable outcomes.
- [LLP 3000.002](./3000.002-end-to-end-resource-bounds.research.md):
  physical resource bounds across requests and replication.
- [LLP 3000.003](./3000.003-one-semantics-and-credible-verification.research.md):
  one semantic contract across Rust, TypeScript and verification hosts.

The [detailed review and 37-ticket index](./reviews/3000-architecture-code-review-20260912.md)
record evidence, alternatives and acceptance examples. All four children are
Draft Research. They remain review findings and proposals; the core owner
retains implementation custody under §1 decision 4.

The author subsequently requested implementation of the 37 concrete tickets.
All 37 are now closed; the [correction qualification record](./reviews/3000-review-corrections-20260913.md)
contains the executed checks, independent reviews and remaining research scope.
Their resolution records and the children's `Implementation follow-up`
sections describe the delivered boundaries: transactional semantic activation,
confirmed-base replay and atomic snapshot ownership, bounded admission/output,
and compiler/client parity corrections. Integration preserves the concurrently
landed native Expo module and ordered-merge performance work. The four children
remain Draft Research for their explicitly outstanding choices; the ticket
fixes do not claim that every architecture question has been settled.

The separate [security review](./reviews/3000-security-review-20260913.md)
opened twelve findings across the HTTP, identity, storage, synchronization and
asset boundaries. Its
[remediation record](./reviews/3000-security-remediation-20260913.md) closes
those findings and three overlapping comprehensive-review findings. The four
security decisions in §7 state the lasting contract; individual tickets retain
the counterexamples, acceptance evidence and limits.

## How to orient

Read this document, then LLP 2000.000 for the argument and its evidence,
then `snapback4/AGENTS.md` once it exists, then the journal. For the
sibling's position read LLP 2000; for the incumbent, LLP 1000; for the
history both inherit, LLP 0329 and its family.

## Non-claims

- This document accepts nothing in LLP 2000.000 on the author's behalf; it
  is the brief, and only running code binds.
- It changes no Snapback 2 or Snapback 3 status, scope, ceiling or gate, and
  edits neither rules file.
- It decides no promotion; the comparison and its ruling are the author's.
- It claims no result; every number above is a threshold or a plan.

## 2026-09-23 — Clique 0.3 follow-up implementation commission

Charlie commissioned Astra max or xhigh to implement the accepted
[modeling plan](./3000.011.000.001-modeling-after-clique-030.plan.md) and
[scale plan](./3000.011.001.001-scale-after-clique-030.plan.md). Their earlier
sanity review found no material concerns. Implementation preserves each plan’s
measurement-first sequence and stopping conditions. Historical research links
3000.000 and 3000.001 leave the current overlay to make room; the documents
remain. Package publication and mutation of the frozen benchmark are outside
this commission.

## 2026-09-27 — Global search beyond one read

Lane GS reproduced the global-search boundary and stopped at a design handoff.
The supervisor narrowed 0.4.6 to compact, bounded one-read membership search;
[LLP 3000.024](./3000.024-global-search-beyond-one-read.rfc.md) records the
Decision, implementation, measurements and explicit grouped-search refusal
beyond its envelope. The retained-session and stateless multi-pass contracts
remain future RFC work. Budgets, disclosure rules and the no-query-cache
exclusion are unchanged; round 1's report records qualification failures.

# LLP 3000.013.001: Wave 12 semantic language gaps

**Type:** Spec
**Status:** Review
**Systems:** Snapback 4 Quarry parser, binder and type system; maintained views; core schema and interpreter; device stores; Lean model; differential generator; authoring guide
**Author:** GPT-5.6 Sol (for Charlie Cheever)
**Date:** 2026-10-14
**Revised:** 2026-10-15 (capture-time provenance and explicit scaled-division contract)
**Related:** [LLP 3000](./3000-snapback4.explainer.md), [LLP 3000.007](./3000.007-relaxed-quarry-and-explicit-residency.rfc.md) §§5.1–5.3, [LLP 3000.013](./3000.013-quarry-states-intent.rfc.md) §§3.2, 5.1, 14.7, [LLP 3000.019](./3000.019-what-the-builder-still-wrote.rfc.md) §3.2

## 1. Decision

Wave 12 adds five small language forms whose shared rule is that convenience
must not invent information. They use the existing interpreter, read-rule,
maintenance and transaction-fact machinery rather than parallel execution
paths.

1. `view total: count t` is a global maintained aggregate. Its group key is
   the empty tuple, so its target contains at most one physical row. Quarry
   reads it as the scalar name `total`. A readable missing row is the count
   identity, zero. A hidden row and a hidden miss are both null. Other
   aggregate kinds may use the same empty key, retaining their existing empty
   result semantics.
2. An exact successful guard `require count(domain) > 0` (or the equivalent
   reversed comparison) proves that the same domain is non-empty until a
   write, a dependent-local rebind, an authority change or a control-flow
   join invalidates the fact. `min` and `max` become present only when their
   projected column is itself present; a non-empty population does not prove
   that an optional column has a non-null value. `sum` keeps its existing zero
   identity. A matching maintained-role guard may make the keyed result
   present through LLP 3000.013 §3.2's existing authority proof, but a write
   invalidates that proof. No guard turns hidden data into zero.
3. `text ... format email` adds deterministic validation, not normalization.
   It accepts ASCII addresses of at most 254 bytes with exactly one `@`; a
   1–64 byte local part made from ASCII alphanumerics and
   ``.!#$%&'*+/=?^_`{|}~-`` (with neither edge nor two adjacent characters
   equal to `.`); and a 1–253 byte dot-separated domain whose 1–63 byte labels
   contain ASCII alphanumerics or interior `-`. A single domain label is
   allowed. Case and bytes are preserved. Everything else refuses `E_INPUT`
   at the ordinary argument/write validation boundary.
4. `search` and `matching` accept enum columns as well as text columns. Enum
   values use their declared string spelling and the existing word
   tokenizer/index. No enum coercion, hidden synonym or alternate ranking is
   added.
5. Rounded division has two explicit forms. `divide(dividend, divisor,
   rounding)` accepts a present `int`, `decimal S` or `money CUR S` dividend
   and a present `int` divisor, and keeps the dividend's type and scale.
   `divide(numerator, denominator, S, rounding)` accepts two present ints, a
   literal result scale `S` from 0 through 12, and returns `decimal S`; this is
   the direct fractional-average form. `rounding` in either form is the
   compiler-checked enum literal `toward-zero`, `away-from-zero`, `floor`,
   `ceiling`, `half-away-from-zero` or `half-even`. Division by zero refuses
   `E_DIV_ZERO`; an unrepresentable final i53 result or checked wide
   intermediate refuses `E_OVERFLOW`. Optional inputs must first be narrowed
   or coalesced; null is never treated as zero. Runtime evaluation uses signed
   integer quotient and remainder only—never floating point.

## 2. Rounded-division table

For the three-argument form let `x` be the dividend's stored integer. For the
four-argument form let `x = numerator * 10^S`, computed in a checked signed
wide integer without first imposing the i53 value bound. Let `q` be `x / d`
truncated toward zero, `r` its remainder, and `d` the divisor. When `r != 0`,
the direction away from zero is the sign of the exact quotient
(`sign(x) * sign(d)`).

| Mode | Adjustment from `q` |
| --- | --- |
| `toward-zero` | none |
| `away-from-zero` | one unit away from zero |
| `floor` | one unit toward negative infinity only for a negative exact quotient |
| `ceiling` | one unit toward positive infinity only for a positive exact quotient |
| `half-away-from-zero` | away only when `2 * abs(r) >= abs(d)` |
| `half-even` | away when greater than half; at an exact half only when `q` is odd |

The comparison is overflow-free in the implementation (it does not compute a
potentially overflowing doubled machine integer). Examples: `divide(5, 2,
'half-even') = 2`, `divide(7, 2, 'half-even') = 4`, `divide(-5, 2,
'half-away-from-zero') = -3`, `divide(5, -2, 'floor') = -3`, and
`divide(5, -2, 'ceiling') = -2`. Decimal and money values apply the same rule
to their exact stored minor-unit integer and retain their scale/currency.
The scaled form gives `divide(101, 2, 2, 'half-even') = 5050` (the decimal-2
value 50.50), `divide(1, 8, 2, 'half-even') = 12`, and
`divide(1, 8, 2, 'half-away-from-zero') = 13`; negative operands round with
the same signed rules. Scale zero is valid. Scale 12 is the maximum. Only the
rounded stored result must fit i53, so a wider intermediate such as
`divide(I53_MAX, I53_MAX, 12, 'half-even')` succeeds with
`1000000000000`.

## 3. Global maintained-view representation

The empty group is a real logical key, not a dummy author-visible column.
Physical stores still need an index component, so the generated target carries
one compiler-owned required boolean discriminator `$global = true`; its unique
`byGroup` index contains only that field. Quarry never exposes the field or
accepts it as a group key. Schema validation permits this exact target shape
only for an ungrouped maintain; user-declared empty indexes remain invalid.
Every aggregate writer uses the existing canonical maintained-row id function
over the logical empty tuple, so rebuild, activation and incremental updates
address the same row.

The scalar name lowers to a point read of that unique discriminator. It is not a
whole-table page and it consumes no arbitrary first row. Device completeness
and absence use that same unique key. Consequently public synced views can
prove readable absence and return zero, while private views retain null unless
the same authority proof that authorizes a keyed target is live.

## 4. Non-empty-domain proof

The binder records a count's canonical read domain at the instant the local
receives the count. That capture marker is owned by both the result local and
every stable value root used by the read, including the transaction-state
anchor. A successful strict-positive guard may publish a fact only while all
of those capture markers remain live. The fact is keyed by the canonical
domain and matches only an identical table, selected index, keys, seeks and
filter expression after stable-value substitution.

Assignments clear the local's facts. Writes clear every count-capture marker,
the transaction anchor and all aggregate non-empty and maintained-authorization
facts. Authority changes clear the same capture markers before a later guard
can publish anything. Branch joins keep only facts present on every completing
path; loops reach the existing binding fixed point. Entering or leaving an
`as` viewer scope does not export the proof. Retained assignment origins remain
diagnostic history and are not provenance by themselves. This deliberately
misses some equivalent predicates; it must never accept a merely similar or
stale population.

The proof affects only static optionality. Runtime aggregates remain subject
to the existing meter/cut rule: no partial scalar escapes a cut. A later
write followed by an empty population therefore cannot reuse the earlier
proof, and an unauthorized maintained miss never becomes an authorized zero.

## 5. Compatibility and fences

`email` is a new closed schema enum tag and rounded division is a new closed IR
builtin tag. Older runtimes fail closed when decoding either. Existing schema
and program bytes are unchanged. Enum search and global views reuse existing
wire forms except for the deliberate one-component, compiler-owned `$global`
discriminator index; schema validation rejects that shape outside its exact
maintained target.

No feature adds JavaScript evaluation, a second interpreter, regular
expressions, floating point, unbounded scans or implicit disclosure.

## 6. Verification contract

Affected tests must cover:

- parsing, binding, schema validation and incremental maintenance of a global
  count, including zero, insert, delete and readable-versus-hidden absence;
- global-view replication and updates in both SQLite and memory device stores;
- matching count guards, mismatched domains, optional projected columns,
  branch joins, result and dependent-root rebindings, stale counts captured
  before writes, impure helper calls, authority edges, and fresh counts after
  writes;
- immediate keyed-role authorization, hidden hit/miss equivalence, readable
  zero, and invalidation by a following write;
- accepted and rejected email edge cases through both server and device
  validation;
- enum search planning, matching and replication on both stores;
- every rounding mode across positive and negative operands, exact halves,
  non-halves, result scales 0 and 12, the direct `101 / 2` decimal-2 average,
  a wide intermediate with a representable result, zero divisors and final
  i53 overflow, with no floating-point oracle;
- Lean evaluation and differential-case generation for rounded division;
  independent Lean witnesses for global-total presence/disclosure,
  count/role narrowing, email validation and enum word search; plus simulator
  and both device-store execution. Model boundaries remain explicit rather
  than reporting unsupported maintained/search execution as oracle coverage.

The guide's types, search, language and derived/maintained-view cards carry
the exact supported spellings and the presence/disclosure boundaries.

# What Snapback 4 Does Not Do

Longer than the doing-list on purpose. Moving something off it means writing one
line naming what it unblocks and taking something off the doing-list in the same
change. Nothing comes off during slice one (LLP 3000 §3: the clock is four weeks).

## The bars

The bakeoff gates in [LLP 3000 §3](../llp/3000-snapback4.explainer.md) are the
bars: the loop bar, hub acquisition within 2× the measured floor, client bytes
under the competitor-anchored budgets, the six scenarios green in the simulator
on the hub fixture. Everything not required to pass them does not exist yet.

## Not building

- **A second authoring language.** Quarry, one spelling, copied from Snapback 2 (LLP 1004, LLP 1007 §4b). TypeScript is for seeds, journeys, effect bodies and the client.
- **Byte- and step-priced certification.** No closure, heap, output or step ceilings at `check`; the certificate certifies shape, uniformity, completeness class and prediction eligibility. Resources are metered at runtime (LLP 2000.000 §6).
- **A per-query result cache.** No range receipts, validators, halos, absence evidence, coverage spans, extent facts, held directories, fact integrity or swap journals. The client replicates declared partitions with one watermark each (LLP 2000.000 §7).
- **Engine-minted ids for client inserts, grants, ID slices.** Ids are client-minted, time-ordered, 128-bit; the engine mints for its own writes.
- **The `drain` protocol, follower-tab inbox rows, `defineItems`, `withHistory`, `mutationTarget`, `receipt.source`, `local.caughtUp`, `partial` reason codes.** The card (`packages/snapback4/src/contract.ts`) is the whole client vocabulary; `why` carries the rest.
- **`retain` as a receipt rule.** `until-revoked` and `delivered-history` survive as the client's leave policy on a scope tombstone.
- **JavaScript in the data plane.** Queries and mutations run in the Rust interpreter inside the SQLite transaction, and in the same interpreter on the client (host decided at week 3 by measurement, LLP 2000.000 §9). A *job* may be the app's own Rust, hosted as wasm through the interpreter's operations under the job's meter (LLP 3000.005, the Moves line below); a query or mutation may not.
- **An embedded JS engine for effects.** The data plane never calls out. `emit` writes the effect durably in the mutation's commit and an external worker holding a service token claims, performs and settles it over the wire (`snapback4/worker`); the four reference effects run on the builder's Node (LLP 2000.000 §10, decided 2026-09-13).
- **Committed generated code; an auth mode; a supervised bundler.** As Snapback 2 (`rules/NOT-DOING.md`): types by inference, personas not modes, `SNAPBACK_URL` is the client contract.
- **A TypeScript twin of the interpreter.** Retired by the author's ruling of 2026-09-13 (LLP 3000 §7): one implementation, in Rust, on every host — the core's SQLite under the native module, memory under wasm on the web with the host persisting each commit's changes. The web pays 312,924 B gzip before the diet where the twin's tier paid 16,842; the conformance obligation between two interpreters ends. Expo Go runs the online tier.
- **More than one hosted target, an admin console, media transcoding or thumbnails, push delivery itself (a worker's), cron syntax beyond `every N at HH:MM`, vector or relevance-ranked search (word-prefix search is in: `words(..)` and `matching`), federation, multi-region, device-to-device sync, at-rest encryption claims.** After the clock, on evidence.
- **General observability beyond `why`, `trace`, `logs`, `data`, `sql`, server operation latency, and bounded client subscription inspection.** The `sql` verb is read-only inspection of the developer's own store (LLP 3000.007 §8.8), never an application surface. Separately declared application SQL reads are admitted for increment D (LLP 3000.009 §6.2, §14.4; the 2026-09-16 Moves line below), not implemented by this inspection verb.
- **A guide corpus.** The compiler's facts, one language card and keyword cards, as Snapback 2; no freshness dates. Every card's Quarry is compiled by the suite and the language card is run, so a card cannot promise a form the parser refuses.

- **Partial union delivery, two group audiences, a public branch.** A union syncs principal-column branches and at most one group branch, each a whole disjunct of the read rule (LLP 3000.036.000.000); a branch with an extra condition, a second group or a `public` branch is refused with the rewrite, never partially delivered; service grants add no device audience (LLP 3000.007 §20.6). A union does not combine with delivered history, read-through children, synced or row-keyed views, owner keys, timelines, private field owners, acquisition windows or direct/inherited expiry on content or membership scopes.
- **Exact horizon maintenance.** The horizon bounds acquisition (a snapshot, a bootstrap); the log never evicts or back-fills to hold a device's group at exactly N rows. A local read past the acquired horizon by key says `complete: false`.

- **Clock expressions in rules.** A read rule compares with `=` and `!=` only; `.expiresAt > now` remains a mutation `require`. Stored lifetimes use `not before .col` for server-controlled opening and `expire at .col` for expiry. The engine publishes opening commits; devices receive no unopened payload. The supported compositions and explicit exclusions are in LLP 3000.036.003.
- **A jobs framework.** No priorities, worker pools or dead letters: one FIFO of runs, one chunk at a time, a refused chunk parked with its refusal. Actions, effects and scheduled actions carry per-operation retries, deadlines and cancellation, and a record per external step (LLP 3000.009 §5.2; the Moves line below). Missed period boundaries while a server is down fire once on return, not once per missed period.
- **Asset processing.** No transcoding, thumbnails, rungs or HEIC: the bytes are served as uploaded; JPEG, PNG, WebP and MP4 only; image dimensions and optional MP4 duration read from the header; MP4 dimensions are not extracted.
- **Search beyond word prefixes.** One tokenizer (NFC, lowercase, alphanumeric words), one index shape (`scope, words(cols), order.., id`), one read (`matching`): every term a prefix of some word, the first term's words merged as lanes, bounded by the meter in a uniform scope, at most 32 words in an admitted non-uniform scope. No stemming, no ranking by relevance, no phrase or mid-word matching, no per-language folding, no intersection index for the second term (it filters the page).
- **An identity provider.** Guests, password accounts, service seats, and sessions a service mints for accounts the application authenticated itself. No phone, OAuth, magic links or MFA in the engine: an application's worker verifies those and mints the session.
- **No multi-server presence, no presence history or last-seen storage, no broadcast event stream, no connection-bound rows.** `connected[p]` stays server-wide, counting held polls and their 35 s grace; a live query that may read it re-runs in the background about every 5 seconds to stay current (LLP 3000.018). Typing and viewing use short-lived, authorized ephemeral tables in one server process (LLP 3000.017); they never sync or decide durable invariants. A device does not know presence and says so.

## Process not doing

- **2026-09-28 — ordinary mixed search** (LLP 3000.028 §3.3, lane OS round 1).
  Admit the SDK's bounded merge of current server rows and certified Left images
  for the proved single time-ordered word page. This removes application-owned
  online/held selection for that shape. One server call per requested page;
  recomputation pays again from consumed positions. The coordinator shares the
  existing device read allowance. No durable search session, extra synced rows,
  new authority or additional allowance is admitted.

- **2026-09-28 — inferred current-scope word search** (LLP 3000.028 §3.3,
  lane OS). A single bare ordinary word page may derive the exact non-expiring
  membership audience for the existing compact server plan. This removes the
  requirement to repeat that audience predicate in this narrow server query.
  The compiler supplies a scoped words index when absent; it is an ordinary
  schema index, maintained on writes and on devices, with no extra synced rows
  or new authority. The device keeps its original bare scan. Round 0 left ordered composition
  and combined accounting outside its independent subset; round 1 above admits
  that bounded SDK composition. Durable search sessions remain excluded.
- **2026-10-12 — union sync audiences** (LLP 3000.036.000.000, workstream UNION, two blind review rounds). A table syncs to the union of principal-column audiences and one group audience; a device holds a row while any branch admits its viewer. What it unblocks: owner-or-team, buyer-or-seller and owner-or-staff tables offline for every reader, the shape three app farms asked for. Taken off: "one `sync` audience per table". Partial delivery, two groups and a public branch stay excluded (above).
- **2026-09-28 — explicit owner-key coverage** (LLP 3000.028 §3.4, Lane OK commission). A public window may opt into a compiler-owned, horizon-free principal key partition to answer ordinary own-key existence offline. Taken off: the exclusion of narrower platform key distribution beside one public window. General union audiences, full own-row replication and per-query absence caches remain excluded. The projection uses existing metered maintains, adoption and whole-partition publication; existing windows do not expand.
- **2026-09-26 — a live query keeps presence current** (LLP 3000.018). A 0.4.2 probe found an observed `connected[p]` reporting `fresh: true` while stale for over a minute, and a comparative builder polled around it. What it unblocks: online marks and presence-aware lists that stay current. Taken off: "a presence stream" as an exclusion for live queries; a presence API, last-seen storage and multi-server presence stay out. Under the author's standing authorization for reworks that make queries excellent (2026-09-25).

- **No drift registries, inventories, line-pinned diagnostics registries, claim digests or frozen-record gates.** Generated projections are built, not committed.
- **No gate bound to an exact commit; no push gated on a battery.** Five blocking checks in sixty seconds, run before push; everything slower on a schedule with bounded tickets.
- **No ceiling raised in slice one.** Budgets are anchored to a competitor's measured number and recorded once in the journal.
- **No standing panel** except the two blind reviews LLP 3000 decision 4 names (partition inversion; the meter's cut on non-uniform scans). The author explicitly commissioned the one-off LLP 3000.008 panel and disjoint Astra implementation lanes on 2026-09-14; its bounded disposition is recorded in that umbrella and LLP 3000 §7.
- **No new LLP number** without first extending LLP 3000 or LLP 2000.000; no spec without running code (`rules/RULES.md`: only implemented specs bind).
- **No second session on the core** during slice one; lanes take the periphery.
- **No test matrix as the primary harness.** The simulator is; real-browser and device witnesses cover only what it cannot reach.
- **No files over 1,500 lines**, including ones copied from Snapback 2. Split on
  the way in. The one exception is the package's published documents
  (`packages/snapback4/README.md` and its linked references): the rule exists to
  keep a *source* file reviewable, and the 0.2.25 calibration measured the cost of
  applying it to a document — splitting the README was that run's single largest
  scoring loss, because the builder then had four places to look instead of one
  (LLP 3000.007 §20.19). A published document is bounded by whether a builder can
  find one way in, not by a line count; the census that enforces this rule covers
  source, and says so.

## Deliberately worse

- No compatibility with Snapback 1 or Snapback 2 apps, packages or on-disk formats.
- No API stability before the clock; every publish is `0.0.x`.
- Sparse prose documentation; the diagnostics and the recipes carry the load.
- One hosted target, SQLite in the production CLI, one platform binary at a time. A separately enabled Postgres evaluation adapter is not a hosted/deployable target (Moves below).

## Moves

- **2026-10-13 — stored lower lifetimes (`not before`) are commissioned by PAGE2** (LLP 3000.036.003). This unblocks timed disclosure without an application publication job. Per-application jobs that flip a publication flag leave this admitted path; clock expressions in rules, captured lower lifetimes for read-through children, and boundary recounts for maintained data remain excluded. HS owns handshake transport; PAGE2 declares the new semantic capability.

- **2026-10-06 — exact Following history (FH delegated round-1 ruling).**
  Admit a Following-specific server traversal and bounded volatile frontier/replay
  state under LLP 3000.026 §9. The approved design reads exact history online
  from the first page; the SDK will own continuation. The first milestone is internal event metadata
  and the oracle, with no public completeness claim. Taken off: a blanket ban on
  state for this one expiring read. General result caches, resumable queries,
  device traversal, inbox coverage certificates and history-only blocks remain
  excluded. Existing inbox bytes and runtime ceilings remain unchanged.

- **2026-09-26 — server operation latency (LLP 3000.019 §3.7).** Calibration #22
  measured each read once and could not claim repeated p95/degradation testing.
  Unblocks percentile measurements of the application's own load without a
  custom timing harness. Taken off: a latency harness written by the builder;
  general metrics, tracing infrastructure and an admin console remain excluded.

- **2026-09-23 — Quarry states intent moves onto the doing-list** (Charlie:
  "if you need to try making *significant* architectural reworks in order to
  make the data modeling, queries, and scalability be excellent and elegant,
  you can experiment with that"; LLP 3000.013). Unblocks reads that state
  predicates and order, one `view` declaration for derived data, Following
  without caps, jobs and aggregates without author-chosen bounds, an app
  object and an offline web shell. Taken off: index names and author-chosen
  engine numbers on the paved path; the timeline's promotion, pull routes and
  caps; the "Time in rules" refusal is unchanged. Stands: one interpreter, no
  JavaScript in the data plane, no lowering Quarry to SQL, no relevance-ranked
  search.

- **2026-09-16 — the remaining general-purpose program moves onto the doing-list**
  (Charlie: "complete everything ... we'll remove services when we are done
  with the workroom test rather than wait to add them in ... use the fleet";
  LLP 3000.009 §14.7). Unblocks the §7 integrated-service baseline, C3 production
  Postgres, D's authorized database capabilities and measurement-led C2/E
  work while Workroom continues. Taken off: waiting for Workroom before
  starting services, and keeping production Postgres integration outside the
  current implementation program. The completed Workroom record drives a
  retain/remove pass. Stands: one interpreter and managed writer, no managed
  SQL writes, no JavaScript transactions, no SQL in the first Workroom cell,
  no invented performance/real-provider evidence, no expo-build-1000 load,
  and no new publication or managed-service deployment authority.

- **2026-09-16 — application SQL reads move onto the doing-list** (Charlie: "admit application SQL reads"; LLP 3000.009 §6.2, §14.4, increment D). Unblocks reporting and search over authorized projection inputs and declared database-owned tables, with authorization before selection, ordering and limits. Taken off in the same change: the inspection-only exclusion for application SQL and D's pending-author gate. Implementation and qualification remain pending; SQLite and Postgres halves ship together, channels follow candidates, and A1–A3 plus the fixture pack stay first. Stands: no SQL writes into managed data, no lowering Quarry to SQL, no SQL in the first Snapback brief cell, and no publication authorization.

- **2026-09-15 — a Postgres evaluation backend moves onto the doing-list** (author: "Build the evaluation prototype"). Unblocks concrete evaluation of storage agnosticism, interpreter/database round trips and transactional sync ordering. This bounded prototype replaces full CLI/deployment integration as the next backend task; those product surfaces remain off this increment's doing-list. It shares Quarry, rules, maintains and partition logic, but uses an experimental Postgres store. Native SQL exists only as an explicitly separate comparison workload, not an application escape hatch. SQLite remains the production and local-device store. See [the experiment](./experiments/postgres/README.md).

- **2026-09-15 — the server half of LLP 3000.009 moves onto the doing-list** (author: "begin implementation using astra xhigh to code"; LLP 3000.009 §10.2, increments A1–A3). Unblocks the online-only brief's invitation, webhook and scheduled journeys without a second application server: work records whose authority is bound at admission, ids bound to their operation and arguments, declared action results, a generated handler context with a durable record per external step, declared routes and webhooks, scheduled actions under service seats, and `serve` supervising the runtime. Taken off in the same change: "A jobs framework" narrows — actions, effects and scheduled actions get per-operation retries, deadlines and cancellation; priorities, worker pools and dead letters stay out — and the actions card's "supervise the worker as an ordinary Node process" leaves. Stands: no JavaScript in the data plane, no identity provider in the engine, and no `ctx.sql`, projections or SQL reads in this increment.

- **2026-09-15 — the Postgres access boundary moves onto the doing-list** (LLP 3000.009 §10.2, increment C1). Unblocks measuring Postgres behind a batched access boundary instead of row-at-a-time calls: transaction-scoped prefetch, a pipelined commit tail, one portable index-key bound checked on both stores, and the corpus, conformance and device suites on both stores. Taken off in the same change: the evaluation prototype's single-adapter framing, in which the only next step was an unbatched store. Stands: Postgres is not a hosted or deployable target; its refusals of assets, jobs, actions and schedules, and `serve`, `deploy` and backup on Postgres, wait for increment C3.

- **2026-09-13 — word search, effects, service seats and presence move onto the doing-list** (author: "add all the things we need for Clique and Interview"; the full-brief Clique record's remaining capability lines and Interview's adapter responsibilities). What it unblocks: Clique M5's three search scopes online and offline on one index, M6's push seam without connection leases hand-built in tables, M8's digest emitted rather than logged into a table, Interview's phone sign-in and model workers as a service that mints sessions instead of proxying every request, and the ranked feed of LLP 3000.004 on the `feeds` card. Taken off in the same change: "an embedded JS engine for effects, until measured" (the engine never calls out; a worker does), "word search" from the package's exclusions. Stands: no JavaScript in the data plane, no relevance ranking, no identity provider in the engine.
- **2026-09-12 — expiry, assets and jobs move onto the doing-list** (author: "complete snapback4 to the point where the entire clique benchmark can be run"). What it unblocks: the struck half of the Clique brief (M4 media, M5 view-once and expiry with no client open, M8's scheduled digest). Taken off in the same change: the "one photo path" and "cron syntax" exclusions above narrow to transcoding and to cron beyond `every N [at HH:MM]`; the "time in rules" exclusion stands, answered by `expire at .col` rather than a rule.
- **2026-09-13 — native jobs on (author: "write up an LLP about this and then implement").** Unblocks the scoring half of a feed — floats, vectors, real loops, a typed schema — without putting any of it into Quarry, and gives the model half of LLP 3000.004 a seat inside the bounds: one chunk per commit, the job ceilings, fuel, no I/O. Off the doing-list in the same change: the "external ranker with a service seat" of LLP 3000.004 §6.5 is no longer the only shape for compute over the store; it remains the shape for the network. Ranked and vector search, effects execution and an embedded JS engine stay above.
- **2026-09-13 — `sql`, a fifth named observability verb, moves onto the doing-list** (author ruling on LLP 3000.007 §14.10). Read-only SQL over generated per-table views of the developer's own store, under `dev` or against a `--release` directory; never rule-filtered, never an application surface. What it unblocks: the ground truth two Clique essays said Snapback lacked beside Postgres ("there is no underlying Postgres to query"), and the inspection the 0.2.6 Astra lane did by copying its SQLite file. Taken off in the same change: SQL reads as an application query surface (LLP 3000.007 §9.1) leave the doing-list until the reporting gap is measured after the language relaxations; reporting is Quarry's, inspection is `sql`'s. Stands: no JavaScript in the data plane, one authoring language.

- **2026-09-14 — the simulator witness is published for app builders (LLP 3000.007 §20.10, S30).** Unblocks native offline proof artifacts; replaces the repository-only Metro witness on the doing-list. Real-browser and device witnesses still cover only what the deterministic simulator cannot reach.

- **2026-09-14 — development clock control (S27, calibration F5)** moves onto the doing-list to unblock expiry and scheduled-digest tests without real-minute waits or backdated seeds; waiting on real time leaves the development test path, while production clock controls stay off the doing-list.

- **2026-09-25 — ephemeral tables enact the standing realtime line of LLP 3000.009 §12 (LLP 3000.017).** Unblocks typing and viewing without durable outbox traffic. Nothing leaves the doing-list: this enacts an already admitted scope. Last-seen storage, multi-server presence, a separate presence stream and ephemeral authority over durable invariants remain excluded.

- **2026-09-30 — bounded client subscription inspection (author's PR round-0 task)**
  moves onto the doing-list: query-group queue/in-flight/withdrawal counts and
  bounded latency/cause samples explain mounted screens flashing empty. Taken off
  the doing-list: builders must reconstruct this pressure from ad-hoc console
  instrumentation. General observability beyond the named verbs remains excluded;
  there is no telemetry transport or server admin console (LLP 3000.023 §10).

# Rules

**Snapback 2 optimizes the edit-to-verified loop — the agent's building an app on it, and ours building it.**

Speed is the constraint. Everything below exists to keep the edit-to-verified loop short
and to stop this repository accreting apparatus faster than anyone can remove it. These
rules bind `snapback2/` and the LLPs numbered 1000 and up; Snapback 1 is baselined, not
exempt, and its numbers may only fall.

Each rule is **[check]** (enforced by `caps`) or **[review]** (enforced by a human).

## Budgets — every budget is a trade, not a limit

- **5 blocking checks, 60s total.** A sixth requires deleting one, in the same PR. **[check]**
- **59 documents in the working set** (`llp/current/`). Today's count. A 60th requires
  archiving one — which is just removing the link. Snapback 2 aims to leave this at 15;
  Snapback 1's links are archived at promotion. **[check]**
- **12 documents in the foundation** (`llp/foundation/`). **[check]**
- **This file: 700 words.** If it grows, something becomes a check or stops being a rule. **[check]**
- **1,500 lines per source file, baseline 204.** Generated files exempt — and generated
  files are built, not committed; so are benchmark run records under `benchmarks/runs/`,
  which are evidence, not source. The baseline is Snapback 1's oversize count on the day
  the rule landed; it may not grow, and `snapback2/` contributes zero to it. **[check]**

## Blocking checks

`caps` · `ref-check` · `snapback2/scripts/test.sh` · `snapback2/scripts/measure.mjs --check` · `check-inventories`

## Loop shape

- **Every check reports all failures in one run.** Fail-fast is banned. **[check]**
- **Nothing blocks on anything slower than 60 seconds.** Slow verification runs on a
  schedule, attributed to the breaking commit, and files a bounded set of issues. **[review]**
- **Fix loops get 3 rounds.** Then stop: escalate to a human or descope. **[review]**

## Time budgets

Tracked every run of `snapback2/scripts/measure.mjs` (appends `snapback2/metrics/loop.jsonl`).
A regression is a P0 with a name on it.

| | budget |
|---|---|
| Incremental rebuild after a one-line edit | 15s |
| Test what you just changed | 30s |
| Blocking gate suite | 60s |
| Full build, warm cache | 5 min |
| **`snapback2 check` on a program (the agent's product latency)** | **1s** |
| **`snapback2 doctor` boot-to-ready** | **0.5s** |

## Scope

- **`NOT-DOING.md` is binding.** Moving something onto the doing-list means writing why
  and taking something off. **[review]**
- **Delete; don't deprecate.** No compat shims or migration paths before promotion. **[review]**
- **Nothing is compiled, transpiled, or generated at runtime that could be built ahead of
  time.** **[review]**
- **A spec needs an implementer and a date, or it isn't written.** **[review]**
- **Only implemented specs bind.** A document with no running code behind it is a
  proposal, and a proposal cannot block a PR or justify a check. **[review]**

## Agents

- **Agents remove apparatus freely and add none.** An agent PR that adds a check, script,
  registry, config file, or design doc needs explicit human approval saying so. **[review]**
- **2026-09-06 session exception:** the user delegated the search implementation
  decisions; one `snapback2-core/build.rs` may generate pinned Unicode tables at
  build time, alongside the existing package build. No new gate, registry,
  runtime generator, or Unicode dependency is authorized by this exception. **[review]**
- **Snapback 2 LLPs are numbered 1000 and up**; `llp-create` must be told. **[review]**
- **Snapback 4 LLPs are numbered 3000 and up** (`snapback4/`, LLP 3000); it adopts these budgets
  and loop shape as its own and keeps its scope in `snapback4/NOT-DOING.md`. **[review]**

## Shared machines

- Warm, named lanes, one per stream of work. Never clone fresh; reuse the worktree.
- **Never `git stash`.** Use a temporary commit.
- Kill only PIDs you recorded at launch. Never by name or pattern.

diff --git a/snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/bind/program/aggregate_facts.rs b/snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/bind/program/aggregate_facts.rs
new file mode 100644
index 0000000000..10002e01ee
--- /dev/null
+++ b/snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/bind/program/aggregate_facts.rs
@@ -0,0 +1,166 @@
+//! Exact non-empty facts established by successful count guards.
+
+use super::*;
+use std::collections::BTreeSet;
+
+pub(super) const NONEMPTY: &str = "$aggregateNonempty";
+/// Capture marker for a local whose current value is an exact `count(domain)`
+/// observation. It lives on the result and every domain root: ordinary
+/// rebinding, a write, an authority edge, or a control-flow meet therefore
+/// destroys stale provenance without repurposing diagnostic `origins`.
+pub(super) const COUNT_CAPTURE: &str = "$aggregateCountCapture";
+
+impl Binder<'_> {
+    /// A non-empty observation survives evaluation of its guard only when the
+    /// complete condition is write-free. Unlike point facts, aggregate facts
+    /// deliberately admit filters and seeks because those are part of the
+    /// exact domain identity recorded below.
+    pub(super) fn aggregate_guard_safe(&self, value: &A) -> bool {
+        match value {
+            A::Name { .. } | A::Focal { .. } | A::Literal { .. } => true,
+            A::Array { items, .. } => items.iter().all(|item| self.aggregate_guard_safe(item)),
+            A::Object { fields, .. } => fields.iter().all(|field| field.value.as_ref().is_none_or(|value| self.aggregate_guard_safe(value))),
+            A::Unary { value, .. } | A::Assert { value, .. } | A::ShapeUse { base: value, .. } => self.aggregate_guard_safe(value),
+            A::Binary { left, right, .. } => self.aggregate_guard_safe(left) && self.aggregate_guard_safe(right),
+            A::Member { base, .. } => self.aggregate_guard_safe(base),
+            A::Call { name, args, .. } => self.catalog.functions.get(name).is_none_or(|function|
+                self.pure_functions.get(&function.name) == Some(&true))
+                && args.iter().all(|arg| self.aggregate_guard_safe(arg)),
+            A::Read(read) | A::Exists { read, .. } | A::Created { read, .. } => {
+                read.computed.values().all(|value| self.aggregate_guard_safe(value))
+                    && read.keys.iter().all(|key| self.aggregate_guard_safe(&key.value))
+                    && read.seeks.iter().all(|seek| self.aggregate_guard_safe(&seek.value))
+                    && read.filter.as_deref().is_none_or(|filter| self.aggregate_guard_safe(filter))
+                    && read.matching.as_deref().is_none_or(|matching| self.aggregate_guard_safe(matching))
+                    && read.range.as_ref().and_then(|range| range.cursor.as_deref()).is_none_or(|cursor| self.aggregate_guard_safe(cursor))
+            }
+            A::Shape { base, fields, .. } => self.aggregate_guard_safe(base)
+                && fields.iter().all(|field| match field {
+                    ShapeField::Field { value, .. } => self.aggregate_guard_safe(value),
+                    ShapeField::Spread { .. } | ShapeField::NamedSpread { .. } | ShapeField::Bare { .. } => true,
+                }),
+            A::If { then, condition, otherwise, .. } => self.aggregate_guard_safe(then)
+                && self.aggregate_guard_safe(condition)
+                && self.aggregate_guard_safe(otherwise),
+            A::Merge(_) | A::Walk { .. } | A::Write { .. } => false,
+        }
+    }
+
+    /// Record only implications that follow from count's nonnegative range.
+    /// The common authored form is `require n > 0`, where `n` was bound from
+    /// `count(domain)`; direct count calls use the same identity.
+    // @ref LLP 3000.013.001#4-non-empty-domain-proof — the fact belongs to the exact domain roots and transaction state.
+    pub(super) fn record_aggregate_nonempty(&mut self, condition: &A, truth: bool) {
+        if !truth { return; }
+        let A::Binary { op, left, right, .. } = condition else { return };
+        let (candidate, threshold, op) = if let Some(n) = literal_int(right) {
+            (left.as_ref(), n, op.as_str())
+        } else if let Some(n) = literal_int(left) {
+            let reversed = match op.as_str() { "<" => ">", "<=" => ">=", ">" => "<", ">=" => "<=", other => other };
+            (right.as_ref(), n, reversed)
+        } else { return };
+        let proves_positive = match op {
+            ">" => threshold >= 0,
+            ">=" | "=" => threshold >= 1,
+            "!=" => threshold == 0,
+            _ => false,
+        };
+        if !proves_positive { return; }
+        let Some((key, roots)) = self.count_key(candidate) else { return };
+        for root in roots {
+            let Some(mut binding) = self.binding(&root).cloned() else { return };
+            binding.fields.insert(key.clone(), Ty::Bool);
+            self.scopes.last_mut().expect("a scope").insert(root, binding);
+        }
+    }
+
+    pub(super) fn aggregate_nonempty(&self, read: &ReadExpr) -> bool {
+        self.aggregate_key(read).is_some_and(|(key, roots)| roots.iter().all(|root|
+            self.binding(root).is_some_and(|binding| binding.fields.get(&key) == Some(&Ty::Bool))))
+    }
+
+    /// Resolve count provenance before rebinding `name`: a count domain may
+    /// itself refer to the old value of that local. Such a self-dependent
+    /// capture cannot be represented by the post-assignment binding and is
+    /// therefore deliberately not narrowed.
+    pub(super) fn count_capture(&self, name: &str, value: &A) -> Option<(Vec<String>, BTreeSet<String>)> {
+        let A::Call { name: call, args, .. } = value else { return None };
+        if call != "count" || args.len() != 1 || self.catalog.functions.contains_key(call)
+            || !self.aggregate_guard_safe(value) { return None; }
+        let read = args.first().and_then(|arg| self.aggregate_domain(arg))?;
+        let (aggregate, roots) = self.aggregate_key(&read)?;
+        if roots.contains(name) { return None; }
+        let capture = vec![COUNT_CAPTURE.into(), aggregate[1].clone()];
+        Some((capture, roots))
+    }
+
+    /// Attach pre-resolved capture-time provenance after assigning `name`.
+    /// `origins` remains diagnostic history; this field fact is the reaching
+    /// definition proof used by a later `require name > 0`.
+    pub(super) fn record_count_capture(&mut self, name: &str, capture: Option<(Vec<String>, BTreeSet<String>)>) {
+        let Some((capture, mut owners)) = capture else { return };
+        owners.insert(name.to_owned());
+        for owner in owners {
+            let Some(mut binding) = self.binding(&owner).cloned() else { return };
+            binding.fields.insert(capture.clone(), Ty::Bool);
+            self.scopes.last_mut().expect("a scope").insert(owner, binding);
+        }
+    }
+
+    fn count_key(&self, value: &A) -> Option<(Vec<String>, BTreeSet<String>)> {
+        let (count_arg, captured_name) = match value {
+            A::Call { name, args, .. } if name == "count" && args.len() == 1
+                && !self.catalog.functions.contains_key(name) => args.first(),
+            A::Name { name, .. } => self.binding(name)?.origins.iter().rev().find_map(|(_, origin)| match origin {
+                A::Call { name, args, .. } if name == "count" && args.len() == 1
+                    && !self.catalog.functions.contains_key(name) => args.first(),
+                _ => None,
+            }),
+            _ => None,
+        }.map(|arg| (arg, match value { A::Name { name, .. } => Some(name.as_str()), _ => None }))?;
+        let result = self.aggregate_key(&self.aggregate_domain(count_arg)?)?;
+        if let Some(name) = captured_name {
+            let capture = vec![COUNT_CAPTURE.into(), result.0[1].clone()];
+            if self.binding(name)?.fields.get(&capture) != Some(&Ty::Bool)
+                || !result.1.iter().all(|root| self.binding(root)
+                    .is_some_and(|binding| binding.fields.get(&capture) == Some(&Ty::Bool)))
+            {
+                return None;
+            }
+        }
+        Some(result)
+    }
+
+    fn aggregate_key(&self, read: &ReadExpr) -> Option<(Vec<String>, BTreeSet<String>)> {
+        if read.range.is_some() || read.matching.is_some() { return None; }
+        let mut roots = BTreeSet::new();
+        let keys = read.keys.iter().map(|key| Some(format!("{:?}:{}", key.column,
+            self.aggregate_value(&key.value, &mut roots)?))).collect::<Option<Vec<_>>>()?;
+        let seeks = read.seeks.iter().map(|seek| Some(format!("{}:{}:{}", seek.column, seek.op,
+            self.aggregate_value(&seek.value, &mut roots)?))).collect::<Option<Vec<_>>>()?;
+        let filter = match read.filter.as_deref() {
+            Some(value) => Some(self.aggregate_value(value, &mut roots)?),
+            None => None,
+        };
+        if roots.is_empty() { roots.insert(pure_calls::CONSTANTS.into()); }
+        let identity = format!("{:?}:{:?}:{:?}:{keys:?}:{seeks:?}:{filter:?}", read.table, read.index, read.order_keys);
+        Some((vec![NONEMPTY.into(), identity], roots))
+    }
+
+    fn aggregate_value(&self, value: &A, roots: &mut BTreeSet<String>) -> Option<String> {
+        match value {
+            A::Focal { .. } => Some("focal".into()),
+            A::Member { base, name, .. } if matches!(base.as_ref(), A::Focal { .. }) => Some(format!("focal.{name}")),
+            A::Literal { .. } | A::Name { .. } | A::Member { .. } => self.stable_value(value, roots).map(|value| value.key()),
+            A::Unary { op, value, .. } => Some(format!("unary:{op}:{}", self.aggregate_value(value, roots)?)),
+            A::Binary { op, left, right, .. } => Some(format!("binary:{op}:{}:{}", self.aggregate_value(left, roots)?, self.aggregate_value(right, roots)?)),
+            A::Call { name, args, .. } => {
+                let function = self.catalog.functions.get(name)?;
+                if self.pure_functions.get(&function.name) != Some(&true) { return None; }
+                Some(format!("call:{name}:{:?}", args.iter().map(|arg| self.aggregate_value(arg, roots)).collect::<Option<Vec<_>>>()?))
+            }
+            A::Array { items, .. } => Some(format!("array:{:?}", items.iter().map(|item| self.aggregate_value(item, roots)).collect::<Option<Vec<_>>>()?)),
+            _ => None,
+        }
+    }
+}

@@ -125,6 +125,7 @@ impl Binder<'_> {

                     return Err(unknown(site, "delete does not yield a value; return a confirmation value after the write", "delete the row, then `return null`"));
                 }
+                let count_capture = self.count_capture(name, value);
                 // @ref LLP 3000.007#5.1 — rebinding preserves the local slot and resets its flow refinement.
                 if let Some(mut binding) = self.binding(name).cloned() {
                     let mut origins = binding.origins.clone();
@@ -147,6 +148,7 @@ impl Binder<'_> {
                     self.scopes.last_mut().unwrap().get_mut(name).unwrap().origins.push((ty, value.clone()));
                     out.push(Stmt::Let { name: runtime, value: expr });
                 }
+                self.record_count_capture(name, count_capture);
                 Ok(())
             }
             S::Require { condition, code, message, site } => {
@@ -462,6 +464,14 @@ impl Binder<'_> {
 
     /// After `require x != null` or inside `if x != null:`, `x` is present.
     pub(super) fn narrow(&mut self, condition: &A, then_branch: bool) {
+        // Aggregate facts may describe filtered domains, so they have a
+        // slightly wider write-free admission check than point facts. Record
+        // them once for the whole, already-evaluated condition: recursively
+        // recording a safe left operand would be stale when a later operand
+        // calls a writeful helper.
+        if self.aggregate_guard_safe(condition) {
+            self.record_aggregate_nonempty(condition, then_branch);
+        }
         self.narrow_inner(condition, then_branch, self.point_guard_safe(condition));
     }

diff --git a/snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/tests/maintains.rs b/snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/tests/maintains.rs

+#[test]
+fn ungrouped_view_is_a_scalar_with_one_hidden_physical_group() {
+    let bound = compile(r#"view total: count posts
+  read <- allow
+  online only
+query totalPosts():
+  return total
+"#);
+    assert!(bound.diagnostics.is_empty(), "{:#?}", bound.diagnostics);
+    assert_eq!(bound.programs[0].result, snapback4_lang::types::Ty::Int);
+    let maintain = &bound.schema.maintains[0];
+    assert!(maintain.by.is_empty());
+    let target = &bound.schema.tables["total"];
+    assert_eq!(target.columns.get("$global"), Some(&snapback4_core::schema::ColumnType::Bool));
+    assert_eq!(target.indexes["byGroup"].columns(), ["$global"]);
+    let encoded = serde_json::to_value(&bound.programs[0].program).unwrap();
+    assert_eq!(encoded["body"][0]["return"]["get"]["index"], "byGroup");
+    assert_eq!(encoded["body"][0]["return"]["get"]["key"], serde_json::json!([true]));
+}
+

     for head in [

@@ -0,0 +1,381 @@
+use snapback4_core::{Context, Engine, Value, WriteState};
+use snapback4_lang::{compile, types::Ty};
+use std::collections::BTreeMap;
+
+fn checked(source: &str) -> snapback4_lang::Bound {
+    let bound = compile(&[("schema.q".into(), source.into())]);
+    assert!(bound.diagnostics.is_empty(), "{:#?}", bound.diagnostics);
+    bound
+}
+
+fn engine(source: &str) -> Engine {
+    let bound = checked(source);
+    Engine::memory(bound.schema, bound.programs.into_iter().map(|program| program.program)).unwrap()
+}
+
+#[test]
+fn global_count_is_zero_then_tracks_inserts_and_deletes() {
+    let source = r#"
+table entries:
+  public
+  insert <- allow
+  delete <- allow
+  online only
+table moderators:
+  principal: principal
+  unique principal
+  read <- .principal = viewer
+  online only
+view total: count entries
+  read <- allow
+  online only
+view privateTotal: count entries
+  read <- exists moderators[viewer]
+  online only
+mutation add(id: id):
+  return insert entries { id }
+mutation remove(id: entries):
+  delete entries[id]
+  return null
+query totalEntries():
+  return total
+query privateEntries():
+  return privateTotal
+"#;
+    let bound = checked(source);
+    let generated = snapback4_lang::emit::typescript(&bound);
+    let native = snapback4_lang::emit::rust::rust(&bound);
+    assert!(!generated.contains("$global") && !native.contains("$global"),
+        "the physical discriminator leaked into generated application types");
+    for expression in ["total first 1 by .id", "total[true]"] {
+        let bound = compile(&[("bad.q".into(), format!("{source}\nquery bad(c: cursor ?):\n  return {expression}\n"))]);
+        assert!(bound.diagnostics.iter().any(|diagnostic| diagnostic.code == "E_QUARRY_TYPE"
+            && diagnostic.message.contains("global maintained scalar")), "{expression}: {:#?}", bound.diagnostics);
+    }
+    let mut engine = engine(source);
+    let query = |engine: &mut Engine| engine.query("totalEntries", Context::new("alice", BTreeMap::new())).unwrap().data;
+    let private = |engine: &mut Engine, viewer| engine.query("privateEntries", Context::new(viewer, BTreeMap::new())).unwrap().data;
+    assert_eq!(query(&mut engine), Value::Int(0));
+    assert_eq!(private(&mut engine, "alice"), Value::Null, "an unauthorized empty global stays hidden");
+    engine.seed([("moderators".into(), snapback4_core::value::object([
+        ("id", Value::Id("mod".into())), ("principal", Value::Id("alice".into())),
+    ]))]).unwrap();
+    assert_eq!(private(&mut engine, "alice"), Value::Int(0), "authorized absence is the count identity");
+    assert_eq!(private(&mut engine, "bob"), Value::Null, "a hidden absence is not zero");
+    for id in ["a", "b"] {
+        let outcome = engine.mutate("add", None, Context::new("alice", BTreeMap::from([("id".into(), Value::Id(id.into()))]))).unwrap();
+        assert!(matches!(outcome.state, WriteState::Sent { .. }), "{outcome:?}");
+    }
+    assert_eq!(query(&mut engine), Value::Int(2));
+    assert_eq!(private(&mut engine, "alice"), Value::Int(2));
+    assert_eq!(private(&mut engine, "bob"), Value::Null, "a hidden stored scalar remains null");
+    engine.mutate("remove", None, Context::new("alice", BTreeMap::from([("id".into(), Value::Id("a".into()))]))).unwrap();
+    assert_eq!(query(&mut engine), Value::Int(1));
+    engine.mutate("remove", None, Context::new("alice", BTreeMap::from([("id".into(), Value::Id("b".into()))]))).unwrap();
+    assert_eq!(query(&mut engine), Value::Int(0));
+    assert!(engine.store().rows("total").unwrap().is_empty(), "zero deletes the physical aggregate row");
+    assert!(engine.store().rows("privateTotal").unwrap().is_empty(), "private zero also has no physical row");
+}
+
+#[test]
+fn matching_count_guard_narrows_only_the_same_required_projection_until_a_write() {
+    let schema = r#"
+table entries:
+  owner: principal
+  score: int
+  optionalScore: int ?
+  by byOwner: owner, id
+  public
+  insert <- allow
+  delete <- allow
+  online only
+"#;
+    let sound = checked(&format!(r#"{schema}
+query stats(owner: principal):
+  n = count(entries[owner] by byOwner)
+  require n > 0 else EMPTY
+  return {{ low: min(entries[owner] by byOwner, .score), high: max(entries[owner] by byOwner, .score), total: sum(entries[owner] by byOwner, .score) }}
+query filtered(owner: principal):
+  require count(entries where .owner = owner) > 0 else EMPTY
+  return min(entries where .owner = owner, .score) + 1
+mutation freshAfterWrite(owner: principal, victim: entries):
+  delete entries[victim]
+  n = count(entries[owner] by byOwner)
+  require n > 0 else EMPTY
+  return min(entries[owner] by byOwner, .score) + 1
+"#));
+    let result = &sound.programs.iter().find(|program| program.program.name == "stats").unwrap().result;
+    assert!(matches!(result, Ty::Object(fields) if fields.values().all(|ty| ty == &Ty::Int)), "{result:?}");
+    assert_eq!(sound.programs.iter().find(|program| program.program.name == "filtered").unwrap().result, Ty::Int);
+
+    for (name, body) in [
+        ("different", "n = count(entries[owner] by byOwner)\n  require n > 0 else EMPTY\n  return min(entries['other'] by byOwner, .score) + 1"),
+        ("optional", "n = count(entries[owner] by byOwner)\n  require n > 0 else EMPTY\n  return min(entries[owner] by byOwner, .optionalScore) + 1"),
+        ("written", "n = count(entries[owner] by byOwner)\n  require n > 0 else EMPTY\n  delete entries[victim]\n  return min(entries[owner] by byOwner, .score) + 1"),
+        ("stale_binding", "n = count(entries[owner] by byOwner)\n  delete entries[victim]\n  require n > 0 else EMPTY\n  return min(entries[owner] by byOwner, .score) + 1"),
+        ("rebound", "n = count(entries[owner] by byOwner)\n  n = 1\n  require n > 0 else EMPTY\n  return min(entries[owner] by byOwner, .score) + 1"),
+        ("joined", "n = count(entries[owner] by byOwner)\n  if flag:\n    n = 1\n  require n > 0 else EMPTY\n  return min(entries[owner] by byOwner, .score) + 1"),
+        ("root_rebound", "who = owner\n  n = count(entries[who] by byOwner)\n  who = next\n  require n > 0 else EMPTY\n  return min(entries[who] by byOwner, .score) + 1"),
+    ] {
+        let args = match name {
+            "written" | "stale_binding" => "owner: principal, victim: entries",
+            "joined" => "owner: principal, flag: bool",
+            "root_rebound" => "owner: principal, next: principal",
+            _ => "owner: principal",
+        };
+        let kind = if matches!(name, "written" | "stale_binding") { "mutation" } else { "query" };
+        let bound = compile(&[("schema.q".into(), format!("{schema}{kind} {name}({args}):\n  {body}\n"))]);
+        assert!(bound.diagnostics.iter().any(|d| d.code == "E_QUARRY_TYPE" && d.message.contains("int ?")), "{name}: {:#?}", bound.diagnostics);
+    }
+
+    let helper = compile(&[("schema.q".into(), format!(r#"{schema}
+fn remove(victim: entries):
+  delete entries[victim]
+  return true
+mutation staleHelper(owner: principal, victim: entries):
+  n = count(entries[owner] by byOwner)
+  removed = remove(victim)
+  require n > 0 else EMPTY
+  return min(entries[owner] by byOwner, .score) + 1
+"#))]);
+    assert!(helper.diagnostics.iter().any(|d| d.code == "E_QUARRY_TYPE" && d.message.contains("int ?")), "{:#?}", helper.diagnostics);
+
+    let authority = compile(&[("schema.q".into(), format!(r#"{schema}
+job staleAuthority():
+  for row in entries:
+    n = count(entries[row.owner] by byOwner)
+    as row.owner:
+      require n > 0 else EMPTY
+      narrowed = min(entries[row.owner] by byOwner, .score) + 1
+"#))]);
+    assert!(authority.diagnostics.iter().any(|d| d.code == "E_QUARRY_TYPE" && d.message.contains("int ?")), "{:#?}", authority.diagnostics);
+
+    let self_dependent = compile(&[("schema.q".into(), r#"
+table buckets:
+  bucket: int
+  score: int
+  by byBucket: bucket, id
+  public
+  online only
+query staleSelfDependent():
+  bucket = 1
+  bucket = count(buckets[bucket] by byBucket)
+  require bucket > 0 else EMPTY
+  return min(buckets[bucket] by byBucket, .score) + 1
+"#.into())]);
+    assert!(self_dependent.diagnostics.iter().any(|d| d.code == "E_QUARRY_TYPE" && d.message.contains("int ?")),
+        "a count result must not inherit the old value's domain provenance: {:#?}", self_dependent.diagnostics);
+
+    let pure_domain = checked(&format!(r#"{schema}
+fn sameOwner(owner: principal):
+  return owner
+query pureDomain(owner: principal):
+  n = count(entries[sameOwner(owner)] by byOwner)
+  require n > 0 else EMPTY
+  return min(entries[sameOwner(owner)] by byOwner, .score) + 1
+"#));
+    assert_eq!(pure_domain.programs.iter().find(|program| program.program.name == "pureDomain").unwrap().result, Ty::Int);
+
+    let impure_domain = compile(&[("schema.q".into(), format!(r#"{schema}
+fn changingOwner(owner: principal, victim: entries):
+  delete entries[victim]
+  return owner
+mutation staleDomain(owner: principal, victim: entries):
+  n = count(entries[changingOwner(owner, victim)] by byOwner)
+  require n > 0 else EMPTY
+  return min(entries[changingOwner(owner, victim)] by byOwner, .score) + 1
+"#))]);
+    assert!(impure_domain.diagnostics.iter().any(|d| d.code == "E_QUARRY_TYPE" && d.message.contains("int ?")),
+        "writeful domains must never be replayed as aggregate proof: {:#?}", impure_domain.diagnostics);
+
+    let shadowed = compile(&[("schema.q".into(), format!(r#"{schema}
+fn count(owner: principal):
+  return 1
+query shadowedCount(owner: principal):
+  n = count(owner)
+  require n > 0 else EMPTY
+  return min(entries[owner] by byOwner, .score) + 1
+"#))]);
+    assert!(shadowed.diagnostics.iter().any(|d| d.code == "E_QUARRY_TYPE" && d.message.contains("int ?")),
+        "a user helper named count must not create builtin provenance: {:#?}", shadowed.diagnostics);
+}
+
+#[test]
+fn matching_role_guard_narrows_only_the_authorized_keyed_total() {
+    let source = r#"
+table entries:
+  owner: principal
+  public
+  online only
+table staff:
+  principal: principal
+  role: enum('finance')
+  unique principal, role
+  read <- .principal = viewer
+  delete <- .principal = viewer
+  online only
+view totals: count entries by .owner
+  read <- exists staff[principal = viewer, role = 'finance']
+  online only
+query raw(owner: principal):
+  return totals[owner]
+query guarded(owner: principal):
+  require exists staff[principal = viewer, role = 'finance'] else NOT_FINANCE
+  return totals[owner] + 1
+"#;
+    let bound = checked(source);
+    assert_eq!(bound.programs.iter().find(|program| program.program.name == "raw").unwrap().result, Ty::Int.optional());
+    assert_eq!(bound.programs.iter().find(|program| program.program.name == "guarded").unwrap().result, Ty::Int);
+    let mut engine = Engine::memory(bound.schema, bound.programs.into_iter().map(|program| program.program)).unwrap();
+    engine.seed([("entries".into(), snapback4_core::value::object([
+        ("id", Value::Id("one".into())), ("owner", Value::Id("bob".into())),
+    ]))]).unwrap();
+    let raw = |engine: &mut Engine, owner: &str| engine.query("raw", Context::new("alice", BTreeMap::from([
+        ("owner".into(), Value::Id(owner.into())),
+    ]))).unwrap().data;
+    assert_eq!(raw(&mut engine, "bob"), Value::Null, "a hidden stored total is not disclosed");
+    assert_eq!(raw(&mut engine, "nobody"), Value::Null, "a hidden miss is indistinguishable from a hidden hit");
+    engine.seed([("staff".into(), snapback4_core::value::object([
+        ("id", Value::Id("finance".into())), ("principal", Value::Id("alice".into())),
+        ("role", Value::String("finance".into())),
+    ]))]).unwrap();
+    assert_eq!(raw(&mut engine, "bob"), Value::Int(1));
+    assert_eq!(raw(&mut engine, "nobody"), Value::Int(0), "an authorized keyed miss has the count identity");
+    assert_eq!(engine.query("guarded", Context::new("alice", BTreeMap::from([
+        ("owner".into(), Value::Id("nobody".into())),
+    ]))).unwrap().data, Value::Int(1));
+
+    let stale = compile(&[("schema.q".into(), format!(r#"{source}
+mutation stale(owner: principal, grant: staff):
+  require exists staff[principal = viewer, role = 'finance'] else NOT_FINANCE
+  delete staff[grant]
+  return totals[owner] + 1
+"#))]);
+    assert!(stale.diagnostics.iter().any(|diagnostic| diagnostic.code == "E_QUARRY_TYPE"
+        && diagnostic.message.contains("int ?")), "a write must invalidate the role proof: {:#?}", stale.diagnostics);
+}
+
+#[test]
+fn email_format_is_one_shared_ascii_contract() {
+    let source = r#"
+table contacts:
+  email: text 3..254 format email
+  public
+  insert <- allow
+  online only
+query echo(email: text 3..254 format email):
+  return email
+"#;
+    let mut engine = engine(source);
+    for email in ["a@example.com", "first.last+tag@sub.example", "x@localhost"] {
+        let result = engine.query("echo", Context::new("alice", BTreeMap::from([("email".into(), Value::String(email.into()))]))).unwrap();
+        assert_eq!(result.data, Value::String(email.into()));
+    }
+    for email in ["a..b@example.com", ".a@example.com", "a@-example.com", "a@example..com", "a@пример.рф", "a@@example.com"] {
+        let error = engine.query("echo", Context::new("alice", BTreeMap::from([("email".into(), Value::String(email.into()))]))).unwrap_err();
+        assert_eq!(error.code, "E_INPUT", "{email}: {error:?}");
+    }
+}
+
+#[test]
+fn enum_columns_use_the_existing_word_search_path() {
+    let source = r#"
+table businesses:
+  category: enum('coffee-shop', 'book_store', 'museum')
+  search category
+  public
+  online only
+query find(q: text <=40, c: cursor ?):
+  return businesses matching q first 20 by .id after c
+"#;
+    let mut engine = engine(source);
+    engine.seed([
+        ("businesses".into(), snapback4_core::value::object([("id", Value::Id("cafe".into())), ("category", Value::String("coffee-shop".into()))])),
+        ("businesses".into(), snapback4_core::value::object([("id", Value::Id("books".into())), ("category", Value::String("book_store".into()))])),
+    ]).unwrap();
+    let result = engine.query("find", Context::new("alice", BTreeMap::from([
+        ("q".into(), Value::String("cof".into())), ("c".into(), Value::Null),
+    ]))).unwrap();
+    let rows = result.data.as_array().unwrap();
+    assert_eq!(rows.len(), 1);
+    assert_eq!(rows[0].field("id").unwrap(), &Value::Id("cafe".into()));
+}
+
+#[test]
+fn rounded_division_is_exact_signed_and_keeps_numeric_types() {
+    let source = r#"
+query rounded():
+  return [
+    divide(5, 2, 'toward-zero'), divide(5, 2, 'away-from-zero'),
+    divide(5, 2, 'floor'), divide(5, 2, 'ceiling'),
+    divide(5, 2, 'half-away-from-zero'), divide(5, 2, 'half-even'),
+    divide(7, 2, 'half-even'), divide(-5, 2, 'half-away-from-zero'),
+    divide(-5, 2, 'floor'), divide(-5, 2, 'ceiling'),
+    divide(5, -2, 'floor'), divide(5, -2, 'ceiling'),
+    divide(7, 3, 'half-even'), divide(8, 3, 'half-even')
+  ]
+query decimalValue(value: decimal 2):
+  return divide(value, 2, 'half-even')
+query moneyValue(value: money USD 2):
+  return divide(value, 2, 'half-even')
+query scaled(numerator: int, denominator: int):
+  return [
+    divide(numerator, denominator, 2, 'half-even'),
+    divide(numerator, denominator, 2, 'half-away-from-zero')
+  ]
+query wide(numerator: int, denominator: int):
+  return divide(numerator, denominator, 12, 'half-even')
+query scaleZero(numerator: int, denominator: int):
+  return divide(numerator, denominator, 0, 'half-even')
+query scaledOverflow():
+  return divide(9007199254740991, 1, 1, 'half-even')
+query zero():
+  return divide(1, 0, 'half-even')
+"#;
+    let bound = checked(source);
+    assert_eq!(bound.programs.iter().find(|p| p.program.name == "decimalValue").unwrap().result, Ty::Decimal(2));
+    assert_eq!(bound.programs.iter().find(|p| p.program.name == "moneyValue").unwrap().result, Ty::Money("USD".into(), 2));
+    let mut engine = Engine::memory(bound.schema, bound.programs.into_iter().map(|p| p.program)).unwrap();
+    let values = engine.query("rounded", Context::new("alice", BTreeMap::new())).unwrap().data;
+    assert_eq!(values, Value::Array([2, 3, 2, 3, 3, 2, 4, -3, -3, -2, -3, -2, 2, 3].into_iter().map(Value::Int).collect()));
+    for name in ["decimalValue", "moneyValue"] {
+        let value = engine.query(name, Context::new("alice", BTreeMap::from([("value".into(), Value::Int(5))]))).unwrap().data;
+        assert_eq!(value, Value::Int(2), "{name}");
+    }
+    let scaled = |engine: &mut Engine, numerator, denominator| engine.query("scaled", Context::new("alice", BTreeMap::from([
+        ("numerator".into(), Value::Int(numerator)), ("denominator".into(), Value::Int(denominator)),
+    ]))).unwrap().data;
+    assert_eq!(scaled(&mut engine, 101, 2), Value::Array(vec![Value::Int(5050), Value::Int(5050)]));
+    assert_eq!(scaled(&mut engine, -101, 2), Value::Array(vec![Value::Int(-5050), Value::Int(-5050)]));
+    assert_eq!(scaled(&mut engine, 1, 8), Value::Array(vec![Value::Int(12), Value::Int(13)]));
+    assert_eq!(scaled(&mut engine, -1, 8), Value::Array(vec![Value::Int(-12), Value::Int(-13)]));
+    assert_eq!(engine.query("wide", Context::new("alice", BTreeMap::from([
+        ("numerator".into(), Value::Int(snapback4_core::value::I53_MAX)),
+        ("denominator".into(), Value::Int(snapback4_core::value::I53_MAX)),
+    ]))).unwrap().data, Value::Int(1_000_000_000_000), "the wide scaled intermediate is not restricted to i53");
+    assert_eq!(engine.query("scaleZero", Context::new("alice", BTreeMap::from([
+        ("numerator".into(), Value::Int(5)), ("denominator".into(), Value::Int(2)),
+    ]))).unwrap().data, Value::Int(2));
+    assert_eq!(engine.query("scaledOverflow", Context::new("alice", BTreeMap::new())).unwrap_err().code, "E_OVERFLOW");
+    assert_eq!(engine.query("zero", Context::new("alice", BTreeMap::new())).unwrap_err().code, "E_DIV_ZERO");
+
+    use snapback4_core::ir::{Builtin, Expr, Program, ProgramKind, Rounding, Stmt};
+    let overflow = Program {
+        name: "overflow".into(), kind: ProgramKind::Query, args: BTreeMap::new(),
+        read_time_granularity_ms: None, native: false, returned_shapes: BTreeMap::new(),
+        body: vec![Stmt::Return(Expr::Builtin { builtin: Builtin::Divide { rounding: Rounding::TowardZero, scale: None },
+            args: vec![Expr::Literal(Value::Int(i64::MIN)), Expr::Literal(Value::Int(-1))] })],
+    };
+    let mut raw = Engine::memory(Default::default(), [overflow]).unwrap();
+    assert_eq!(raw.query("overflow", Context::new("alice", BTreeMap::new())).unwrap_err().code, "E_OVERFLOW");
+
+    for bad in ["divide(1, 2, 'nearest')", "divide(1, 2, mode)", "divide(1, divisor, 'half-even')",
+        "divide(1, 2, 13, 'half-even')", "divide(1, 2, scale, 'half-even')"] {
+        let source = if bad.contains("mode") { format!("query bad(mode: enum('half-even')):\n  return {bad}\n") }
+            else if bad.contains("scale") { format!("query bad(scale: int):\n  return {bad}\n") }
+            else if bad.contains("divisor") { format!("query bad(divisor: decimal 2):\n  return {bad}\n") }
+            else { format!("query bad():\n  return {bad}\n") };
+        let bound = compile(&[("bad.q".into(), source)]);
+        assert!(bound.diagnostics.iter().any(|d| d.code == "E_QUARRY_TYPE"), "{bad}: {:#?}", bound.diagnostics);
+    }
+}
