### 3.2 Maintained views: aggregates

```quarry
view followerCount: count follows by .followedId
view receivedLikes: count likes by .postId.authorId
view dailyPosts: count posts where not .removed by .authorId, day(.at)
view revenue: sum orders of .amount by .shopId

table posts:
  likeCount: count likes
```

These have exactly the semantics of today's maintains: the same engine, the
same recorded attribution, and maintained columns for the in-table form.
`followerCount[p]` reads the number, 0 when absent. Two defaults remove the
boilerplate lines:

- **Readability is derived.** A uniformly readable source gives a readable
  aggregate. A source whose read rule uses only bare group keys gives the same
  rule over the aggregate's key. Anything else needs an explicit `read <-`.
- **In-table columns find their reference.** A column is counted by the one
  column of the source that references the table. If there are several, use
  `count likes by .postId`.

### 3.3 Materialized views: Following
### 5.1 Streaming aggregates

`count(R)`, `sum(R, .col)` and `exists R`, where `R` is a predicate read
without a page clause, examine rows under the examined and steps meters.
Each decoded row is charged transiently, and exactly that charge is released
when the row is dropped. Retained state stays charged.

A streaming consumer keeps the underlying scan's admission, authorization,
expiry and coverage checks. Calling a scan `count` does not make it a safe
probe; LLP 3000.007 §21.9's expiring-mutation counterexample stands.
Unpaged aggregate reads retain ordinary streaming scans: membership, enum
complements and window predicates do not become bounded merge pages. Their
residual filters fold the entire admitted domain, or return null on a cut.
Streaming aggregation across planned lanes is not implemented.

The busy-chat digest claim (LLP 3000.011.001.000's 16 MiB stop) holds only
if the retained recipient-heavy fixture passes.

An inferred aggregate seek must preserve predicate short-circuiting: its
endpoint must already be present and must not fail when evaluated before the
whole predicate. Otherwise the comparison stays a residual filter. Selecting
one safe endpoint must not extract an unsafe sibling bound on the same column.
A named prefix pinning intrinsic `id` keeps its single-row access path; remaining
comparisons filter that row rather than introducing an order after the id pin.

### 5.2 Self-chunking jobs
### 14.7 A hidden keyed maintained value is unavailable, including at zero

A keyed maintained point authorizes the requested group before disclosing
whether storage contains it. If admitted, a stored group returns its value and
an absent group returns the maintained operation's identity, currently zero
for `count` and `sum`. If the point's read rule hides the group, a stored group
and an absent group both return `null`; the absent case must never fall through
the authorized-miss coercion and become zero. A query may instead `require` the
same authority condition first and return its chosen refusal code.

The authorization decision is identical for stored and synthetic groups even
when the aggregate's source expires. The actual system principal bypasses the view
rule for a synthetic miss just as it does for a stored hit, and therefore sees
the identity. That policy bypass does not bypass a read-through parent's
lifetime: an inherited group remains nullable until an exact live-parent point
witness proves the parent exists. A row yielded by a fresh table read can carry
that witness through a loop only when the whole loop body is proven write-free;
saved or buffered row values do not renew it, and a writeful loop needs a
current point guard in each iteration.
Because an unguarded private point can return `null`, its static result is a
nullable number. A matching effect-safe authority proof may narrow that result
to the maintained operation's required numeric type. Maintained-rule focal
fields stay scoped to the maintained target row; only declared group fields
are replaced with caller key expressions, so an unrelated caller projection
cannot supply a target-only rule field. A
write, relevant rebinding, or entry into and exit from a viewer-scoped block
invalidates that proof; a job inside `as principal` has that principal's
authority rather than the system exemption. Loop headers retain only facts
that survive every iteration, including facts on an outer binding while a
loop item shadows its source name, separately from local assignment revisions.
Program `exists` observes only rows readable by its current viewer, while an
`exists` witness inside the maintained rule tests raw presence below the
target table's read rule. A positive program witness therefore proves raw
presence, but a negative one proves raw absence only for the actual system
scope, a uniformly readable target, or a complete point key whose selected row
is structurally readable. Matching text alone does not turn a hidden miss into
a raw-absence authority proof. Authored rule constants are resolved before
group-key expressions are substituted. A parameter, local or loop item named
`allow`, `public` or `deny` remains a lexical operand rather than acquiring the
rule constant's meaning.

`check` emits `N_MAINTAIN_AUTHORIZATION` when a query directly returns a bare,
fully keyed maintained point whose read rule is narrower than the set of query
callers and no dominating `require` proves the same authority condition for
the current key and authority scope.
An unrelated requirement does not silence it. The note names the three
observable states—value, authorized zero and hidden `null`—and recommends the
explicit `require`. A regression exercises both a stored and absent group for
an authorized caller, an unauthorized caller, and a second principal admitted
by an `exists` branch of the view rule.

### 5.1 The seven forms (rev 1)

| # | Today | Proposed | Source | Bound by |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 1 | A local binds once ("duplicate local") | `x = expr` rebinds a local of the same type; a `for` may carry a counter or a running sum | S | the step meter; `Stmt::Assign` exists |
| 2 | No accumulators | `xs = []` then `xs = xs + [v]` (the binder emits `Push`); `s = s + n`; a list built in a loop is bounded by the heap meter and may be iterated | S | the heap meter (`Push` charges) and the step meter |
| 3 | `continue` refused | `continue` | S (minimalism) | `Stmt::Continue` exists |
| 4 | `else if` refused | `else if cond:` as sugar for the nesting (`elif` is a guess `check --fix` rewrites, §5.5) | S (minimalism) | nothing to bound |
| 5 | `where` refused | `t[prefix] [after c] first\|last N by idx where <predicate>`: the scan proceeds in index order; a row passing the read rule and the predicate counts toward N; examined rows and probes are metered | S + A's completeness class | the examined-rows and probe meters; §5.6 fixes completeness and cursors |
| 6 | Text `+` refused | `text + text` → `Concat`; `slice`, `tokens`, `count(text)`, `lower(text)`; no `format`, no interpolation | S (bytes) and a product discipline | the output and heap meters |
| 7 | `/` and `%` refused | `a / b` (integer quotient) and `a % b` beside `divmod`; zero refuses `E_DIV_ZERO` | S (a semantic preference) | nothing to bound |

**2026-09-27 — list expressions (0.4.5 lane QE).** `a + b` concatenates
two lists in every expression position, including returns, arguments and
record fields. Item types join as in list literals: incompatible nonempty
items refuse immediately, including references to different tables and
conflicts nested inside records or lists. Empty lists supply no competing
item type. Compatible scalar items retain a common representation. Two enums
join to their deduplicated variant union, including subsets and equivalent sets
in different orders; an existing superset keeps its order, otherwise the union
follows encounter order. Quarry imposes no variant-count limit. Self-append
checks both operands against the joined item type, so disjoint enums also
keep their original string values. Enum and text widen to text bounded by both
the longest variant and the text operand,
under the existing text character-domain rule. If an enum item cannot join,
the diagnostic teaches a typed identity helper such as
`fn state(v: enum('on', 'off')): v`, with all variants, and
`[state(a), state(b)]` when every value belongs to that enum; this preserves
values without conversion. Generic id with a reference or
principal becomes id; int with decimal or money retains that numeric type
without rescaling; json accepts other known, non-row values. Unknown is never
a widening candidate in a nonempty item join. A mixed-reference refusal teaches
the intentional loss of table specificity: `fn exposedId(value: id): value`,
then `[exposedId(x), exposedId(y)]`, or `exposedId(row.id)` in each projected
field for a nested `[].id` conflict. An effect's `once [recipient, period]` is a positional key tuple,
not an iterable list, so its coordinates may differ in type. Bounds add, saturating
at the existing meter-bound sentinel. The shared `Concat` builtin charges
the result allocation. `xs = xs + [x]` retains its in-place `Push` lowering.
Other loop-carried concatenations retain the existing heap-bound widening
when their item type stabilizes; recursive item growth refuses with a
self-append rewrite using a fixed item type. A list plus a non-list names
both operand types and suggests `xs + [x]`. The recursive-growth rewrite
also covers concatenations within conditional assignments and failures caught
at reassignment, after the assigned expression typechecks; an ill-typed `+`
keeps its operand diagnostic without a recursive-growth cascade. Consuming
the accumulator in a scalar, such as `xs = xs + [count(xs)]`, also keeps the
ordinary item-join diagnostic; only embedding the accumulator in the resulting
container earns recursive-growth guidance.
The length census prices bounded append and prepend increments
like `Push`, including the initial contents; doubling and unresolved cyclic or
open growth retain the step-ceiling fallback. No runtime ceiling changes.

### 5.2 Composition: helpers with bodies, jobs with preludes

**8. `fn` takes a body.** Today `fn` is one expression and `proc` is a
block that returns nothing, so the 0.1.0 builder wrote the receipt
fan-out three times (`send`, `sendMedia`, `sendPoll`) and the 0.2.6
builder wrote its receipt state as three nested one-line `fn`s. LLP
2000.000 §6 named the split as pricing residue. Proposed: one `fn`,
declared with an expression or with a block ending in `return`, callable
in expression position anywhere; expanded inline as helpers are today
(`bind/program/control.rs`), so a write inside a body is admitted exactly
when the caller may write. **Loop placement also inherits the caller's
enclosing loops:** a helper inside a job's recipient sweep performs ordinary
per-recipient loops; a helper at the job's top level still receives the
automatic-sweep and stable-domain checks. `break` and `continue` remain
lexically scoped to loops inside the helper. A query calling a writing `fn`
is a placement error at the call site, with the site named. `proc` is deleted with the
rewrite `fn` (rules: delete, don't deprecate).

```quarry
fn receiptState(cid: conversations, sender: principal, seq: int):
  unread = 0
  undelivered = 0
  for m in memberships[cid] first 50 by byConversationPrincipal:
    if m.principal = sender or m.joinedSequence >= seq:
      continue
    if m.readSequence < seq:
      unread = unread + 1
    if m.deliveredSequence < seq:
      undelivered = undelivered + 1
  return 'read' if unread = 0 else ('delivered' if undelivered = 0 else 'sent')
```

**2026-09-27 — repeated pure calls (0.4.5 lane QE, calibration #22).**
A null test narrows a repeated `fn` call like a field path of a lexical
snapshot when the expanded helper reads no table, writes nothing and reads
neither `now` nor another changing input (the commit/read clock, a minted id
or connection state). Every argument must be a lexical name, a field path
of a lexical snapshot, a literal, or another such pure call. For example,
`now + windows.ttl(c.disappearing) if windows.ttl(c.disappearing) != null else null`
compiles. A database read used directly as an argument does not qualify;
binding its result once creates a lexical snapshot.

The null fact identifies the helper and ordered arguments, including each
snapshot's lexical identity. It is scoped to the guarded branch, short circuit
or surviving guard and invalidated when any argument root is reassigned or
carried through a changing loop. The binder checks the expanded IR, including
transitive helpers and named shapes, for effects; it never removes a read,
write or clock access to make a call pure. Both calls still execute in source
order under the existing meters. Every other optional call keeps the existing
located `E_QUARRY_TYPE` diagnostic and “bind the optional call once” rewrite.

**2026-10-03 — guarded point reads (lane QE2, calibration #30).**
`require exists t[k]`, `require t[k] != null`, and the present branch of
`if t[k] != null` prove the identical unique-key lookup non-null, including
helper arguments. The binder records stable key identities in the current
lexical state; rebindings, writes (including expanded expression helpers),
branch joins, loop fixed points and job viewer changes invalidate stale facts. Guards containing
writing helpers establish no point fact. Both reads still execute and are
metered. This does not make a table-reading helper pure.

A required reference is a typed id, not an existence constraint. Even when a
parent's read rule is exactly the membership probe just required, a membership
can outlive an absent parent. No parent-existence implication is inferred.
The nullable helper-argument diagnostic names `require exists
conversations[conversation] else NOT_FOUND` or a bind with `! else NOT_FOUND`.
Enum-to-text widening already admits every variant whose character length
fits the destination bounds; nullable enums still need their own null guard.

**9. A job may have a prelude.** Today a job's body is `chunk` sweeps and
nothing else (`parser/programs.rs:73`); both 0.2.6 builders hit it
("jobs must start with chunk"; "job-level binding outside a chunk").
Proposed: statements before the first `chunk` form a prelude that runs at
the start of every chunk under that chunk's meter — argument-derived
bindings, a config row, `divmod` of the period — with its bindings
visible in every chunk. Re-running it per chunk keeps a chunk wholly
done or not started; a prelude that writes is refused (a write belongs
to a chunk).

### 5.3 Data access: seeks, groups, a page is a list

**10. Range seeks in the key.** The IR and the runner already scan a
value range on the first unpinned index column (`Scan.range`,
`read.rs:381`); Quarry has no spelling for it, so a builder who needs
"messages at or after this one" or "posts in the trailing day" scans
from the start of the prefix and filters, examining everything older.
Proposed: a comparison on the first unpinned column inside the key
brackets is a seek — `t[prefix, col >= x]`, `t[prefix, col > a, col <=
b]` — priced as a prefix, continued by a cursor as any page is. A `where`
on that column alone is accepted and lowered to the same seek, with a
note naming the seek spelling so the cost is visible.

```quarry
query around(conversationId: conversations, messageId: messages):     -- M3: tapping a quote jumps to the original
  q = messages[messageId] ! else NOT_FOUND
  before = messages[conversationId, at < q.at] last 20 by byConvTime
  after = messages[conversationId, at >= q.at] first 21 by byConvTime
  return { before, after }

query trending():                                                     -- M8: the top ten of the trailing day
  day = posts[at > now - 86400000] first 4095 by byTime { id, at, likes: likeCounts[.id] }   -- the candidate bound also leaves room for count lookups
  return slice(sort(day, '-likes', '-at'), 0, 10)
```

A ranking is a ranking of the rows it examined. Rev 1 of this example
ranked `last 200` — the newest two hundred of the day — and the received
review caught that it could omit the day's most-liked post. The corrected
form seeks at most 4,095 candidates, leaving room for count lookups in the
shared examined budget (§20.21). A larger day returns accurately scored prefix
winners with `complete: false`; an unfinished count or heap cut returns
`data: null, complete: false`. A `sort`, `group`, `sum` or `count` over a page with a
`next` cursor is over that page, and `check --cost` prints it as such.
For a population that outgrows the ceiling the maintained ranking on the
`feeds` card — `ranked`, scored by a job on a cadence — is the recipe, and
it is what the 0.2.6 lane built.

**11. `group`, `first`, `last`, `at`.** Grouping is the one shape neither
accumulators nor sorting give, and Clique asks for it three times
(global search grouped by chat, reactions counted per emoji, receipts by
stage); the value model has no dynamic map, so a builder groups on the
client. Proposed builtins, all over lists and pages, heap-metered:
`group(list, 'col')` → `[{ key, items }]` in first-seen order;
`first(list)`, `last(list)`, `at(list, i)` → the element or null;
`join(list, sep)` and `trim(text)` beside `lower(text)`, the small text
library the digest and the push preview keep asking for. A typed `map` is
considered and deferred (§11).

```quarry
query reactionCounts(messageId: messages):
  m = messages[messageId] ! else NOT_FOUND
  rows = reactions[m.conversationId, messageId] first 50 by byConvMessage
  return group(rows, 'emoji') { emoji: .key, count: count(.items), mine: exists reactions[messageId, viewer] }
```

**12. A page is a list.** `sort`, `slice`, `count`, `sum`, `group`,
`first`, `last`, `at` and `for` accept a page, a list, a shaped
collection and a list argument alike; the three types unify for every
list-valued builtin. This is LLP 2000.000 §6's page-to-list gap, closed.

### 5.4 Writes and schema: patching, defaults

     2	use super::{Expr, Stmt, Value};

     4	const FALLS_THROUGH: u8 = 1;
     5	const BREAKS: u8 = 2;

     7	// A refusal, like a return, cannot reach any later use of a local.

    10	#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
    11	pub(super) struct Outcomes(u8);

    13	impl Outcomes {
    14	    // @ref LLP 3000.007#5.1 — loop transfers carry local types; later statements cannot erase those paths.
    15	    pub(super) fn block(statements: &[Stmt]) -> Self {
    16	        let mut outcomes = FALLS_THROUGH;
    17	        for statement in statements {
    18	            if outcomes & FALLS_THROUGH == 0 { break; }
    19	            // Only paths still falling through execute the next statement.
    20	            outcomes = (outcomes & !FALLS_THROUGH) | Self::statement(statement);
    21	        }
    22	        Self(outcomes)
    23	    }
    24	
    25	    fn statement(statement: &Stmt) -> u8 {
    26	        match statement {
    27	            Stmt::Return(_) => RETURNS,
    28	            Stmt::Break => BREAKS,
    29	            Stmt::Continue => CONTINUES,
    30	            Stmt::Require { condition: Expr::Literal(Value::Bool(false)), .. } => RETURNS,
    31	            Stmt::Require { .. } => FALLS_THROUGH | RETURNS,
    32	            Stmt::If { then_body, else_body, .. } => Self::block(then_body).0 | Self::block(else_body).0,
    33	            // The loop may be empty or finish. Its own break/continue paths
    34	            // do not transfer out of the enclosing loop; returns still do.
    35	            Stmt::For { body, .. } => FALLS_THROUGH | (Self::block(body).0 & RETURNS),

    37	            | Stmt::Push { .. } | Stmt::Check { .. } | Stmt::InsertIfAbsent { .. }

    31	    fn adjacent_assignments(&self, name: &str, left: &A, right: &A) -> bool {

    38	    // Coalescing, helpers, containers and internal joins get an explanation,
    39	    // never source edits hoisted out of their evaluation context.
    40	    pub(super) fn join_error_many(&self, error: JoinError, origins: &[(Ty, A)]) -> Diagnostic {
    41	        refusal(error, origins.last().unwrap().1.site(), String::new())

    43	
    44	    fn simple_arms(&self, error: &JoinError, a: &Ty, b: &Ty, left: &A, right: &A) -> Option<(String, String, Ty, Ty)> {
    45	        if !error.path.is_empty() || !simple_source(left) || !simple_source(right) || a.row_kind().is_none() || b.row_kind().is_none() {
    46	            return None;
    47	        }
    48	        let normalize = |ty: &Ty, source: &A| {
    49	            let fields = self.row_fields(ty, source.site()).ok()?.into_iter().collect();
    50	            let normalized = Ty::RowObject(fields);
    51	            Some(if ty.is_optional() { normalized.optional() } else { normalized })
    52	        };
    53	        let left_ty = normalize(a, left)?;
    54	        let right_ty = normalize(b, right)?;
    55	        // A top-level kind conflict can hide a second conflict in its fields.
    56	        // Do not suggest a repair that would simply fail again one level down.
    57	        left_ty.join(&right_ty).ok()?;
    58	        Some((spread(left, a), spread(right, b), left_ty, right_ty))

    61	    pub(super) fn return_type(&self) -> Fallible<Ty> {
    62	        let mut joined: Option<Ty> = None;
    63	        // A provably empty list (`return []`) beside pages of one table is an
    64	        // empty page of it: the result keeps `Page` (and api.ts its `rowKey`),
    65	        // as in the guard form `if grant: return page` / `return []`.
    66	        // @ref LLP 3000.036#15.4 — the guard's empty arm keeps the page's row identity.
    67	        let pages: Vec<&String> = self.returns.iter().filter_map(|(ty, _)| match ty { Ty::Page(table, _) => Some(table), _ => None }).collect();
    68	        let one_table = !pages.is_empty() && pages.iter().all(|table| *table == pages[0]);
    69	        for (ty, _) in &self.returns {
    70	            if one_table && matches!(ty, Ty::List(_, 0)) { continue; }
    71	            joined = Some(match joined {
    72	                Some(prior) => prior.join(ty).map_err(|error| self.join_error_many(error, &self.returns))?,
    73	                None => ty.clone(),

    80	pub(super) fn local(name: &str, site: &Site) -> A { A::Name { name: name.into(), site: site.clone() } }

    82	fn simple_source(expression: &A) -> bool {
    83	    matches!(expression, A::Name { .. } | A::Read(_))
    84	        || matches!(expression, A::Assert { value, .. } if matches!(value.as_ref(), A::Read(_)))
    85	}
    86	
    87	fn spread(source: &A, ty: &Ty) -> String {
    88	    let text = print::expression(source);
    89	    if ty.is_optional() {
    90	        // Shape evaluates its base once and skips its fields when absent;
    91	        // reading the wrapper's field preserves that null. The selected
    92	        // conditional/reassignment site keeps ownership of the whole read.
    93	        format!("(({text}) {{ rowCopy: {{ ... . }} }}).rowCopy")
    94	    } else { format!("{{ ...({text}) }}") }
    95	}
    96	
    97	fn description(kind: &str) -> &str {
    98	    match kind {
    99	        "Row" => "a row (Row)", "WriteRow" => "a write result (WriteRow)",
   100	        "RowShape" => "a row shape (RowShape)", "RowObject" => "a spread object (RowObject)",
   101	        "Object" => "a plain object (Object)", other => other,
   102	    }
   103	}
   104	
   105	fn refusal(error: JoinError, site: &Site, rewrite: String) -> Diagnostic {
   106	    let path = if error.path.is_empty() { String::new() } else { format!(" at field path {}", error.path) };
   107	    if error.incompatible_items {
   108	        let rewrite = if error.mixed_references {
   109	            "use compatible item types; to expose references as generic ids, declare `fn exposedId(value: id): value` and use `[exposedId(x), exposedId(y)]`; for projected IDs (including field path [].id), use `exposedId(row.id)` in each id field"
   110	        } else if error.enum_items {
   111	            "use compatible item types; if every value is an enum variant, declare a typed helper such as `fn state(v: enum('on', 'off')): v` with all variants and use `[state(a), state(b)]` (or wrap each conflicting field); this preserves values without conversion; keep other incompatible types in separate lists"
   112	        } else {
   113	            "use compatible item types in one list; keep different types in separate lists"
   114	        };
   115	        return type_error(site, format!("cannot join list items{path}: {} and {}", error.left, error.right),
   116	            rewrite);
   117	    }
   118	    Diagnostic::new(site, "E_QUARRY_SHAPE_JOIN",
   119	        format!("cannot join{}: arm A is {}, arm B is {}; make both arms the same kind (spread both row arms, or shape both with the same shape)", path, description(&error.left), description(&error.right)),
   120	        rewrite).guide("shapes")
   121	}
   122	
   123	// Normalizing an initial RowShape changes its wildcard rule. Only offer
   124	// assignment edits when no intervening use can observe that changed kind.
   125	fn adjacent(body: &[S], name: &str, left: &A, right: &A) -> bool {
   126	    let assigns = |statement: &S, source: &A| matches!(statement,
   127	        S::Bind { names, value, .. } if names.len() == 1 && names[0] == name && value == source);
   128	    let mentions = |expr: &A| print::expression(expr).split(|c: char| !c.is_alphanumeric() && c != '_').any(|word| word == name);
   129	    if mentions(right) { return false; }
   130	    body.windows(2).any(|pair| {
   131	        if !assigns(&pair[0], left) { return false; }
   132	        if assigns(&pair[1], right) { return true; }
   133	        matches!(&pair[1], S::If { condition, then_body, else_body, .. }
   134	            if !mentions(condition) && ((then_body.len() == 1 && else_body.is_empty() && assigns(&then_body[0], right))
   135	                || (else_body.len() == 1 && then_body.is_empty() && assigns(&else_body[0], right))))
   136	    }) || body.iter().any(|statement| match statement {
   137	        S::If { then_body, else_body, .. } => adjacent(then_body, name, left, right) || adjacent(else_body, name, left, right),
   138	        S::As { body, .. } | S::For { body, .. } => adjacent(body, name, left, right),
   139	        _ => false,
   140	    })
   141	}

     9	    pub(super) fn is_page(&self, read: &ReadExpr, point: ReadMode) -> Fallible<bool> {
    10	        if !read.order_keys.is_empty() || read.range.is_some() || read.matching.is_some() || read.filter.is_some() || !read.seeks.is_empty() || read.keys.is_empty() { return Ok(true); }

    13	        let mut probe = self.clone();
    14	        let info = probe.table(&read.table, &read.site)?;
    15	        let mut lookup = read.clone();
    16	        if point == ReadMode::FilteredPoint && info.maintained.is_some() && lookup.index.is_none() { lookup.index = Some("byGroup".into()); }
    17	        match probe.select_index(info, &lookup, point, &mut setup::Body::default()) {
    18	            Ok(_) => Ok(false),
    19	            Err(error) if error.code == "E_QUARRY_AMBIGUOUS" => Err(error),

    27	                    Err(error) if error.code == "E_QUARRY_AMBIGUOUS" => Err(error),

    35	    pub(super) fn prefix_bound(&self, read: &ReadExpr, index: &ast::IndexDecl, position: Position) -> Fallible<(ast::RangeSpec, Bound)> {
    36	        if let Some(range) = &read.range { return Ok((range.clone(), Bound::Literal(range.take))); }
    37	        let ceiling = if self.caller_kind == ProgramKind::Job { snapback4_core::Ceilings::job() } else { snapback4_core::Ceilings::default() }.examined;
    38	        let pinned = &index.columns[..read.keys.len()];
    39	        // One group per distinct source key: a parent-scoped source cap bounds
    40	        // its aggregate rows too, without copying a constraint onto the view.
    41	        // A pinned unique key bounds the group as a cap does (wave 3 #7); `.id = x`
    42	        // plans as the closed seek `.id >= x and .id <= x` on the next column.
    43	        let mut equal = pinned.to_vec();
    44	        if let Some(next) = index.columns.get(read.keys.len()) {
    45	            let printed = |op: &str| read.seeks.iter().filter(|seek| &seek.column == next && seek.op == op).map(|seek| crate::print::expression(&seek.value)).collect::<Vec<_>>();
    46	            let (low, high) = (printed(">="), printed("<="));
    47	            if read.seeks.len() == 2 && low.len() == 1 && low == high { equal.push(next.clone()); }
    48	        }
    49	        let cap = self.schema.group_bound(&read.table, pinned).into_iter().chain(crate::bind::unique_bound::unique_bound(self.schema, &read.table, &equal)).min().or_else(|| {
    50	            let m = self.schema.maintain_for(&read.table)?;

    70	            if let Some(Some(by)) = &window

    77	            let suggestion = format!("page with `{} N` and a cursor: `{}` (declare `c: cursor ?`)", if window.is_some() { "last" } else { "first" }, self.catalog.source.expression(&A::Read(page.clone())));

    16	#[test]
    17	fn global_count_is_zero_then_tracks_inserts_and_deletes() {
    18	    let source = r#"
    19	table entries:
    20	  public
    21	  insert <- allow
    22	  delete <- allow
    23	  online only
    24	table moderators:
    25	  principal: principal
    26	  unique principal
    27	  read <- .principal = viewer
    28	  online only
    29	view total: count entries
    30	  read <- allow
    31	  online only
    32	view privateTotal: count entries
    33	  read <- exists moderators[viewer]
    34	  online only
    35	mutation add(id: id):
    36	  return insert entries { id }
    37	mutation remove(id: entries):
    38	  delete entries[id]
    39	  return null
    40	query totalEntries():
    41	  return total
    42	query privateEntries():
    43	  return privateTotal
    44	"#;
    45	    let bound = checked(source);
    46	    let generated = snapback4_lang::emit::typescript(&bound);
    47	    let native = snapback4_lang::emit::rust::rust(&bound);
    48	    assert!(!generated.contains("$global") && !native.contains("$global"),
    49	        "the physical discriminator leaked into generated application types");
    50	    for expression in ["total first 1 by .id", "total[true]"] {
    51	        let bound = compile(&[("bad.q".into(), format!("{source}\nquery bad(c: cursor ?):\n  return {expression}\n"))]);
    52	        assert!(bound.diagnostics.iter().any(|diagnostic| diagnostic.code == "E_QUARRY_TYPE"
    53	            && diagnostic.message.contains("global maintained scalar")), "{expression}: {:#?}", bound.diagnostics);
    54	    }

    56	    let query = |engine: &mut Engine| engine.query("totalEntries", Context::new("alice", BTreeMap::new())).unwrap().data;
    57	    let private = |engine: &mut Engine, viewer| engine.query("privateEntries", Context::new(viewer, BTreeMap::new())).unwrap().data;
    58	    assert_eq!(query(&mut engine), Value::Int(0));

codex
I found two material concerns in the committed code: bare global totals appear to reach page classification before the synthetic point key is added, and a fresh count appears able to restore a stale count’s proof after a domain-root rebind. I’m checking the surrounding paths before finalizing the findings.

     1	//! A prefix that pins a unique key bounds its rows as a `cap` does: at most
     2	//! one row, or one per value of the key's remaining finite columns (`unique
     3	//! subscriber, topic` with a three-value `topic` enum: three rows per
     4	//! subscriber). The store enforces the key on every insert and update, so
     5	//! the bound is exact, like a cap's.

     7	use snapback4_core::schema::{ColumnType, Schema};
     8	
     9	/// The tighter of the schema's cap over `pinned` and a unique key's bound.
    10	pub(crate) fn group_bound(schema: &Schema, table: &str, pinned: &[String]) -> Option<u32> {
    11	    schema.group_bound(table, pinned).into_iter().chain(unique_bound(schema, table, pinned)).min()
    12	}
    13	
    14	/// Rows a prefix pinning `pinned` can hold under some unique key of `table`.
    15	pub(crate) fn unique_bound(schema: &Schema, table: &str, pinned: &[String]) -> Option<u32> {
    16	    let info = schema.table(table)?;
    17	    let column = |name: &str| if name == "id" { Some(&ColumnType::Id) } else { info.columns.get(name) };
    18	    info.indexes.values().filter(|index| index.unique && index.is_plain()).filter_map(|index| {
    19	        let columns = index.columns();
    20	        // A nullable key column admits tied nulls; and an unpinned column
    21	        // bounds the group only through its finite set of values.
    22	        let mut rows: u32 = 1;
    23	        for name in &columns {
    24	            let kind = column(name)?;
    25	            if kind.is_optional() { return None; }
    26	            if pinned.contains(name) { continue; }
    27	            let values = match kind {
    28	                ColumnType::Enum { variants } => u32::try_from(variants.len()).ok()?,
    29	                ColumnType::Bool => 2,
    30	                _ => return None,
    31	            };
    32	            rows = rows.checked_mul(values)?;
    33	        }
    34	        (!columns.is_empty()).then_some(rows)
    35	    }).min()
    36	}

//! Bounded abstract values. These budgets belong only to the optional note;
//! they neither admit programs nor charge the engine's meter.
use std::{cell::Cell, collections::BTreeMap, cmp::Ordering, ops::Deref, rc::Rc};

pub(super) const AMBIENT: u8 = 1;
pub(super) const READ: u8 = 2;
pub(super) const ARGUMENT: u8 = 4;
pub(super) const UNKNOWN: u8 = 8;
pub(super) const VALUE_NODES: usize = 4096;
pub(super) const TOTAL_WORK: usize = 262_144;
const VALUE_DEPTH: usize = 128;
const ANALYSIS_DEPTH: usize = 128;

// One shared witness is enough to explain caller selection; it never grows a
// path history. Data, cardinality and control all participate in helper-cache keys.
#[derive(Clone, Default, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, PartialOrd, Ord)]
pub(super) struct Control {
    pub(super) bits: u8,
    pub(super) choice: Option<Rc<(String, String)>>,
}
impl Control {
    pub(super) fn union(&self, other: &Self) -> Self {
        Self { bits: self.bits | other.bits, choice: match (&self.choice, &other.choice) {
            (Some(a), Some(b)) => Some(a.min(b).clone()),
            (a, b) => a.clone().or_else(|| b.clone()),
        } }
    }
}

#[derive(Default, Debug)]
pub(super) struct Budget {
    work: Cell<usize>,
    exhausted: Cell<bool>,
    peak: Cell<usize>,
    widened: Cell<usize>,
    depth: Cell<usize>,
}

pub(super) struct Frame(Rc<Budget>);
impl Drop for Frame { fn drop(&mut self) { self.0.depth.set(self.0.depth.get() - 1); } }

impl Budget {
    pub(super) fn enter(self: &Rc<Self>) -> Option<Frame> {
        if self.depth.get() == ANALYSIS_DEPTH { self.exhausted.set(true); }
        if !self.spend(1) { return None; }
        self.depth.set(self.depth.get() + 1);
        Some(Frame(self.clone()))
    }
    pub(super) fn spend(&self, count: usize) -> bool {
        if self.exhausted.get() || count > TOTAL_WORK - self.work.get() {
            self.exhausted.set(true);
            return false;
        }
        self.work.set(self.work.get() + count);
        true
    }
    pub(super) fn exhausted(&self) -> bool { self.exhausted.get() }
    #[cfg(test)]
    pub(super) fn stats(&self) -> (usize, usize, usize) { (self.peak.get(), self.work.get(), self.widened.get()) }
    pub(super) fn scalar(self: &Rc<Self>, data: u8) -> Value { self.make(data, data, BTreeMap::new(), None, false, Control::default()) }
    pub(super) fn literal(self: &Rc<Self>) -> Value { self.make(AMBIENT, AMBIENT, BTreeMap::new(), None, true, Control::default()) }
    pub(super) fn object(self: &Rc<Self>, data: u8, fields: BTreeMap<String, Value>) -> Value { self.make(data, AMBIENT, fields, None, false, Control::default()) }
    pub(super) fn list(self: &Rc<Self>, cardinality: u8, item: Value) -> Value { self.make(0, cardinality, BTreeMap::new(), Some(item), false, Control::default()) }
    fn make(self: &Rc<Self>, data: u8, cardinality: u8, fields: BTreeMap<String, Value>, item: Option<Value>, literal: bool, control: Control) -> Value {
        let children = || fields.values().chain(item.iter());
        let nodes = 1 + children().map(|value| value.nodes).sum::<usize>();
        let depth = 1 + children().map(|value| value.depth).max().unwrap_or(0);
        // Count expanded nodes even though the representation shares subtrees.
        // The check precedes allocating the parent, so sharing is not the bound.
        let node = if nodes > VALUE_NODES || depth > VALUE_DEPTH || !self.spend(nodes) {
            self.widened.set(self.widened.get() + 1);
            Node { control: Control::default(), data: UNKNOWN, cardinality: UNKNOWN, all: UNKNOWN, nodes: 1, depth: 1, fields: BTreeMap::new(), item: None, literal: false }
        } else {
            self.peak.set(self.peak.get().max(nodes));
            Node { control, cardinality, all: children().fold(data | if item.is_some() { cardinality } else { 0 }, |data, value| data | value.all), data, nodes, depth, fields, item, literal }
        };
        Value { node: Rc::new(node), budget: self.clone() }
    }
}

#[derive(Clone, Debug)]
pub(super) struct Value { node: Rc<Node>, budget: Rc<Budget> }
#[derive(Debug, PartialEq, Eq, PartialOrd, Ord)]
pub(super) struct Node {
    pub(super) data: u8,
    pub(super) cardinality: u8,
    pub(super) control: Control,
    pub(super) fields: BTreeMap<String, Value>,
    pub(super) item: Option<Value>,
    pub(super) literal: bool,
    pub(super) nodes: usize,
    depth: usize,
    all: u8,
}
impl Deref for Value { type Target = Node; fn deref(&self) -> &Node { &self.node } }
impl PartialEq for Value { fn eq(&self, other: &Self) -> bool { Rc::ptr_eq(&self.node, &other.node) || self.node == other.node } }
impl Eq for Value {}
impl PartialOrd for Value { fn partial_cmp(&self, other: &Self) -> Option<Ordering> { Some(self.cmp(other)) } }
impl Ord for Value { fn cmp(&self, other: &Self) -> Ordering { if Rc::ptr_eq(&self.node, &other.node) { Ordering::Equal } else { self.node.cmp(&other.node) } } }

impl Value {
    pub(super) fn all(&self) -> u8 { self.all }
    /// How many: a collection's cardinality bits (its own data bits for a scalar).
    pub(super) fn cardinality(&self) -> u8 { self.cardinality }
    pub(super) fn controls(&self) -> Control {
        // Children are bounded, but charge this traversal as well.
        if !self.budget.spend(1) { return Control { bits: UNKNOWN, choice: None }; }
        self.fields.values().chain(self.item.iter()).fold(self.control.clone(), |control, value| control.union(&value.controls()))
    }
    pub(super) fn summary(&self) -> Self { self.budget.scalar(self.all()).selected(&self.controls()) }
    pub(super) fn field(&self, name: &str) -> Self {
        self.fields.get(name).cloned().unwrap_or_else(|| self.budget.scalar(if self.data == 0 { UNKNOWN } else { self.data })).selected(&self.control)
    }
    pub(super) fn element(&self) -> Self { self.item.clone().map_or_else(|| self.clone(), |item| item.selected(&self.control)) }
    // @ref LLP 3000.007#21.8 — L28: consuming a collection consumes its cardinality separately from its item data.
    pub(super) fn consumed_element(&self) -> Self { self.element().influenced(self.cardinality) }
    pub(super) fn with_cardinality(&self, cardinality: u8) -> Self {
        self.budget.make(self.data, cardinality, self.fields.clone(), self.item.clone(), self.literal, self.control.clone())
    }
    pub(super) fn join(&self, other: &Self) -> Self { self.combine(other, false) }
    pub(super) fn widen(&self, other: &Self) -> Self { self.combine(other, true) }
    fn combine(&self, other: &Self, widen: bool) -> Self {
        if !self.budget.spend(self.nodes + other.nodes) { return self.budget.scalar(UNKNOWN); }
        if self == other { return self.clone(); }
        if widen && self.fields.is_empty() && self.item.is_none() && (!other.fields.is_empty() || other.item.is_some()) {
            return self.budget.scalar(UNKNOWN);
        }
        // A list joined with an object has no single projection semantics.
        if self.item.is_some() && !other.fields.is_empty() || other.item.is_some() && !self.fields.is_empty() {
            return self.budget.scalar(UNKNOWN);
        }
        let mut fields = BTreeMap::new();
        let missing_left = self.budget.scalar(if self.data == 0 { UNKNOWN } else { self.data });
        let missing_right = self.budget.scalar(if other.data == 0 { UNKNOWN } else { other.data });
        for (name, left) in &self.fields {
            if self.budget.exhausted() { return self.budget.scalar(UNKNOWN); }
            fields.insert(name.clone(), left.combine(other.fields.get(name).unwrap_or(&missing_right), widen));
        }
        for (name, right) in &other.fields {
            if self.budget.exhausted() { return self.budget.scalar(UNKNOWN); }
            if !self.fields.contains_key(name) { fields.insert(name.clone(), missing_left.combine(right, widen)); }
        }
        let item = match (&self.item, &other.item) {
            (None, None) => None,
            (left, right) => Some(left.as_ref().unwrap_or(&self.budget.scalar(self.data))
                .combine(right.as_ref().unwrap_or(&self.budget.scalar(other.data)), widen)),
        };
        self.budget.make(self.data | other.data, self.cardinality | other.cardinality, fields, item, self.literal && other.literal, self.control.union(&other.control))
    }
    pub(super) fn selected(&self, control: &Control) -> Self {
        if control.bits == 0 { return self.clone(); }
        // Unsupported dependence remains UNKNOWN data, even if encountered in
        // a selector: the audit did not prove how that construct uses its input.
        let value = if control.bits & UNKNOWN != 0 { self.influenced(UNKNOWN) } else { self.clone() };
        self.budget.make(value.data, value.cardinality, value.fields.clone(), value.item.clone(), value.literal, value.control.union(control))
    }
    pub(super) fn influenced(&self, data: u8) -> Self {
        if data == 0 { return self.clone(); }
        if !self.budget.spend(self.nodes) { return self.budget.scalar(UNKNOWN); }
        let fields = self.fields.iter().map(|(name, value)| (name.clone(), value.influenced(data))).collect();
        let item = self.item.as_ref().map(|value| value.influenced(data));
        self.budget.make(self.data | data, self.cardinality | data, fields, item, false, self.control.clone())
    }
}

   384	            self.sites = sites;

//! Aggregate consumers may choose a seek order; ordinary pages keep their order.
use super::{catalog::{Catalog, TableInfo}, intent, intent_walk};
use crate::ast::{ArgDecl, Expr, FunctionBody, FunctionDecl, Literal, ReadExpr};
use std::collections::{BTreeMap, BTreeSet};

// @ref LLP 3000.013#5.1 — choose an access path, leaving aggregate scan
// admission and streaming to the consumer's existing binder/runtime contract.
#[derive(Clone, Default)]
pub(super) struct Contexts {
    pub domains: BTreeSet<String>,
    pub extrema: BTreeMap<String, String>,
    required: BTreeMap<String, BTreeSet<String>>,
}
impl Contexts {
    pub fn safe(&self, read: &ReadExpr, value: &Expr) -> bool {
        safe_endpoint(value, self.required.get(&read.site.to_string()).unwrap_or(&BTreeSet::new()))
    }
    fn visit(&mut self, value: &mut Expr, tables: &BTreeMap<String, TableInfo>, functions: &BTreeMap<String, FunctionDecl>, required: &BTreeSet<String>) {
        if let Expr::Read(read) | Expr::Exists { read, .. } = value {
            self.required.insert(read.site.to_string(), required.clone());
        }
        if let Expr::Call { name, args, .. } = value
            && is_consumer(name, args, tables) && !functions.contains_key(name)
            && let Some(Expr::Read(read)) = args.first() && read.range.is_none() {
            self.domains.insert(read.site.to_string());
            if matches!(name.as_str(), "min" | "max") && let Some(field) = args.get(1).and_then(intent::column) {
                self.extrema.insert(read.site.to_string(), field.into());
            }
        }
        consumer(value, tables, functions, required);
    }
}
pub(super) fn order(catalog: &mut Catalog) -> Contexts {
    let mut contexts = Contexts::default();
    for doc in &mut catalog.documents {
        for program in &mut doc.programs {
            let required = required_args(&program.args);
            let mut visit = |value: &mut Expr| contexts.visit(value, &catalog.tables, &catalog.functions, &required);
            intent_walk::statements(&mut program.prelude, &mut visit);
            intent_walk::statements(&mut program.body, &mut visit);
        }
        for function in &mut doc.functions {
            let required = required_args(&function.args);
            let mut visit = |value: &mut Expr| contexts.visit(value, &catalog.tables, &catalog.functions, &required);
            match &mut function.body {
                FunctionBody::Expression(value) => intent_walk::expression(value, &mut visit),
                FunctionBody::Block(body) => intent_walk::statements(body, &mut visit),
            }
        }
        for shape in &mut doc.shapes {
            intent_walk::shape(&mut shape.fields, &mut |value| contexts.visit(value, &catalog.tables, &catalog.functions, &BTreeSet::new()));
        }
    }
    contexts
}

// @ref LLP 3000.019#3.2 — defer overload selection to the typed binder.
// Optional filters and inferred lanes may materialize an ordinary read before
// then. Keep the unpaged domain as an alternative for a scalar projection;
// a binary collection comparison still receives its original page plan.
pub(super) fn remember_domains(catalog: &mut Catalog) {
    for doc in &mut catalog.documents {
        intent_walk::document(doc, &mut |expr| {
            if let Expr::Call { name, args, .. } = expr
                && matches!(name.as_str(), "min" | "max") && args.len() == 2
                && !catalog.functions.contains_key(name) && intent::column(&args[1]).is_none()
                && let Expr::Read(read) = &args[0]
                && read.range.is_none() && read.matching.is_none() && catalog.tables.contains_key(&read.table) {
                catalog.extremum_domains.insert(read.site.to_string(), read.clone());
            }
        });
    }
}

fn required_args(args: &[ArgDecl]) -> BTreeSet<String> {
    args.iter().filter(|arg| !arg.type_.optional).map(|arg| arg.name.clone()).collect()
}

pub(super) fn is_consumer(name: &str, args: &[Expr], tables: &BTreeMap<String, TableInfo>) -> bool {
    if matches!(name, "count" | "sum") { return true; }
    if !matches!(name, "min" | "max") { return false; }
    let field = args.get(1).and_then(intent::column);
    // @ref LLP 3000.019#3.2 — expression extrema use ordinary aggregate scan planning.
    // Collection-valued second arguments keep binary page comparison planning.
    if field.is_none() && args.get(1).is_none_or(may_be_collection) { return false; }
    // A binary comparison of two pages keeps their observable row order.
    // A focal member absent from this table can belong to an outer shape.
    match args.first() {
        Some(Expr::Read(read)) => tables.get(&read.table).is_some_and(|table|
            extremum_domain(read, table) && field.is_none_or(|field| field == "id" || table.columns.contains_key(field))),
        _ => true,
    }
}

// Before type binding, only select an aggregate order for a definitely scalar
// result. Otherwise leave the source order intact for the binary overload;
// the binder can still admit a scalar aggregate over that ordinary scan.
fn may_be_collection(value: &Expr) -> bool {
    match value {
        Expr::Literal { .. } | Expr::Unary { .. } | Expr::Exists { .. } | Expr::Created { .. } => false,
        Expr::Binary { op, left, right, .. } if op == "??" || op == "+" => may_be_collection(left) || may_be_collection(right),
        Expr::Binary { .. } => false,
        Expr::If { then, otherwise, .. } => may_be_collection(then) || may_be_collection(otherwise),
        Expr::Assert { value, .. } => may_be_collection(value),
        // Calls can be user helpers, including helpers which return pages.
        _ => true,
    }
}

fn extremum_domain(read: &ReadExpr, table: &TableInfo) -> bool {
    if !read.order_keys.is_empty() || read.range.is_some() || read.matching.is_some()
        || read.filter.is_some() || !read.seeks.is_empty() || read.keys.is_empty() { return true; }
    // Decide the argument's kind before adding an aggregate order. In
    // particular, a complete maintained group lookup is a scalar total.
    // Positional keys without an index retain their point/refusal path;
    // only the binder can resolve their types or diagnose an ambiguity.
    let named = read.keys.iter().all(|key| key.column.is_some());
    if !named && read.index.is_none() { return false; }
    let index = read.index.as_deref().or_else(|| table.maintained.as_ref().map(|_| "byGroup"));
    !table.indexes.iter().any(|candidate| candidate.unique && candidate.is_plain()
        && index.is_none_or(|name| candidate.name == name)
        && (candidate.columns.len() == read.keys.len()
            || named && table.total_unique(candidate) && candidate.columns.len() < read.keys.len())
        && (!named || candidate.columns.iter().all(|column| read.keys.iter().any(|key| key.column.as_ref() == Some(column)))))
}

fn consumer(value: &mut Expr, tables: &BTreeMap<String, TableInfo>, functions: &BTreeMap<String, FunctionDecl>, required: &BTreeSet<String>) {
    match value {
        Expr::Call { name, args, .. } if is_consumer(name, args, tables) && !functions.contains_key(name) => {
            choose_consumer(name, args, tables, required);
        }
        Expr::Exists { read, .. } => choose(read, tables, required),
        _ => {},
    }
}

pub(super) fn choose_consumer(name: &str, args: &mut [Expr], tables: &BTreeMap<String, TableInfo>, required: &BTreeSet<String>) {
    if !is_consumer(name, args, tables) { return; }
    let field = if matches!(name, "min" | "max") { args.get(1).and_then(intent::column).map(str::to_owned) } else { None };
    let Some(Expr::Read(read)) = args.first_mut() else { return; };

impl Requests {
    /// Plan a merge lane: an equality on its order column stays a seek, so
    /// every lane keeps the merge's shared order.
    pub(super) fn plan_lane(&mut self, tables: &BTreeMap<String, TableInfo>, read: &mut ReadExpr) {
        let prior = std::mem::replace(&mut self.lane, true);
        self.read(tables, read, false);
        self.lane = prior;
    }
    pub(super) fn error_count(&self) -> usize { self.errors.len() }

    pub(super) fn branch_index<'a>(&'a self, table: &'a TableInfo, read: &ReadExpr) -> Option<&'a IndexDecl> {
        let name = read.index.as_ref()?;
        self.indexes.get(&(table.decl.name.clone(), name.clone()))
            .or_else(|| table.indexes.iter().find(|index| &index.name == name))
    }

    pub(super) fn branch_index_fits(&self, table: &TableInfo, read: &ReadExpr) -> bool {
        let Some(name) = &read.index else { return false };
        if let Some(index) = self.indexes.get(&(table.decl.name.clone(), name.clone())) {
            // A pending structural token is renamed after planning. Bound
            // the emitted label, including its column names and collision suffix.
            super::key_bound::plain_index_fits(table, index, &index_name(table, index))
        } else {
            table.indexes.iter().find(|index| &index.name == name)
                .is_some_and(|index| super::key_bound::plain_index_fits(table, index, &index.name))
        }
    }

    /// Physical components in the selected plain index, including a pending
    /// structural request before its token receives the emitted index name.
    pub(super) fn branch_index_width(&self, table: &TableInfo, read: &ReadExpr) -> Option<usize> {
        self.branch_index(table, read).filter(|index| index.is_plain()).map(|index| index.columns.len())
    }

    fn index(&mut self, table: &TableInfo, columns: Vec<String>, words: Option<(usize, Vec<String>)>, site: &Site, exact: bool) -> String {
        self.index_computed(table, columns, words, BTreeMap::new(), site, exact)
    }

    fn index_computed(&mut self, table: &TableInfo, columns: Vec<String>, words: Option<(usize, Vec<String>)>, computed: BTreeMap<String, Expr>, site: &Site, exact: bool) -> String {
        let computed_key = computed_descriptor(table, &computed);
        let mut matching = table.indexes.iter().filter(|index| computed_descriptor(table, &index.computed) == computed_key && index.words == words
            && if exact { index.columns == columns } else { index.columns.starts_with(&columns) }).collect::<Vec<_>>();
        matching.sort_by_key(|index| &index.name);
        if exact && matching.len() > 1 {
            self.errors.push(crate::diag::ambiguous(site, format!("{} has several declared indexes for the same planned read: {}", table.decl.name, matching.iter().map(|index| index.name.as_str()).collect::<Vec<_>>().join(", ")),
                format!("choose `by {}` explicitly or keep one definition", matching[0].name)));
    // Reads already inside a merge (a union view's branches) are its lanes.
    let mut merged = std::collections::BTreeSet::new();
    let mut search_variants = std::collections::BTreeSet::new();
    for doc in &mut catalog.documents {
        walk::document(doc, &mut |value| {
            if let Expr::Merge(_) = value {
                let mut copy = value.clone();
                walk::expression(&mut copy, &mut |inner| if let Expr::Read(read) = inner { merged.insert(read.site.to_string()); });
            }
            // @ref LLP 3000.028#3.3 — empty needles retain the original browse arm; the paired scan form also proves nonempty inferred search.
            if let Expr::If { condition, otherwise, site, .. } = value
                && super::intent_variants::condition(condition).is_some_and(|(kind, _)| kind == super::intent_variants::SEARCH) {
                let mut browse = *otherwise.clone();
                walk::expression(&mut browse, &mut |leaf| {
                    if let Expr::Read(read) = leaf && read.site == *site
                        && tables.get(&read.table).is_some_and(|table|
                            matches!(super::intent_branches::read_rule(table), Some(Expr::Exists { .. }))) {
                        search_variants.insert(site.to_string());
                    }
                });
            }
        });
    }
    let mut requests = Requests { contexts, merged, ..Default::default() };
    // Resolve against declared provenance before requests add any access index.
    let mut audiences: BTreeMap<_, _> = tables.iter().filter(|(_, table)| table.decl.sync.is_some())
        .map(|(name, table)| (name.clone(), super::intent_sync::needs(table, catalog))).collect();
    for table in catalog.tables.values_mut() {
        let original = &tables[&table.decl.name];
        for cap in &table.decl.caps {
            if cap.by.iter().all(|column| original.columns.contains_key(column)) {
                requests.index(original, cap.by.clone(), None, &cap.site, false);
            }
        }
        if let Some((column, site)) = &table.decl.expire {
            requests.index(original, vec![column.clone()], None, site, false);
        }
        for rule in &mut table.decl.rules {
            walk::expression(&mut rule.value, &mut |value| {

                requests.index(&tables[&target], columns, None, &sync.site, false);

            // declared index can name; the named index then orders the window inside each group.

                && prefix.iter().any(|column| original.captures.contains_key(column))
                && let Some(declared) = original.indexes.iter().find(|index| &index.name == name)
                && declared.is_plain() && !declared.columns.starts_with(&prefix) {
                let mut columns = prefix.clone();
                for column in &declared.columns { if !columns.contains(column) { columns.push(column.clone()); } }
                if !columns.iter().any(|column| column == "id") { columns.push("id".into()); }
                *name = requests.index(original, columns, None, &sync.site, true);

    27	}

    43	/// Application input is checked before engine slots are prepared. The final
    44	/// stored image must also pass validate_row, with every required slot present.
    45	pub(super) fn validate_before_slots(schema: &Schema, table_name: &str, row: &Value) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
    46	    validate_image(schema, table_name, row, None, true, false)
    47	}
    48	
    49	fn validate_image(schema: &Schema, table_name: &str, row: &Value, viewer: Option<&str>, before_slots: bool, cache: bool) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
    50	    let table = schema.table(table_name).ok_or_else(|| RuntimeError::Schema(format!("missing table {table_name}")))?;
    51	    let object = row.as_object()?;
    52	    if !object.contains_key("id") {
    53	        return Err(RuntimeError::Schema(format!("{table_name} row lacks id")));
    54	    }
    55	    if let Some(name) = object.keys().find(|name| !table.columns.contains_key(*name)) {
    56	        return Err(RuntimeError::Input(format!("{table_name} row contains undeclared field {name}")));
    57	    }
    58	    for (name, kind) in &table.columns {
    59	        if before_slots && crate::schema::timeline::is_slot(name) { continue; }
    60	        if cache && matches!(name.as_str(), "value" | "recountPending") && !object.contains_key(name) { continue; }
    61	        let value = object.get(name).or_else(|| table.maintained.contains_key(name).then_some(&Value::Int(0)));
    62	        if value.is_none() && viewer.is_some_and(|viewer| table.written_by.get(name).is_some_and(|authority|
    63	            !authority.audience && row.field(&authority.owner).ok() != Some(&Value::Id(viewer.into())))) { continue; }
    64	        validate_column(table_name, name, kind, value)?;
    65	    }
    66	    for constraint in &table.constraints {
    67	        if let Constraint::Text { field, min, format } = constraint
    68	            && let Some(Value::String(text)) = object.get(field)
    69	        {
    70	            validate_text(&format!("{table_name}.{field}"), text, *min, *format).map_err(|error| RuntimeError::Constraint(error.to_string().trim_start_matches("E_INPUT: ").to_owned()))?;
    71	        }
    72	    }
    73	    Ok(())
    74	}
    75	
    76	pub fn validate_column(table_name: &str, name: &str, kind: &ColumnType, value: Option<&Value>) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
    77	    let label = format!("{table_name}.{name}");
    78	    if let Some(value) = value {
    79	        crate::value::validate_identities(value)?;
    80	        if contains_system_principal(value) {
    81	            return Err(RuntimeError::Input(format!("{label} cannot carry the reserved system principal")));
    82	        }
    83	    }
    84	    if let Some(code_point) = disallowed_control(kind, value) {
    85	        return Err(RuntimeError::Input(format!("{label} contains disallowed control character U+{code_point:04X}")));
    86	    }
    87	    validate_column_minimum(kind, value, &label)?;
    88	    if !fits(kind, value) {
    89	        return Err(RuntimeError::Input(match value {
    90	            None | Some(Value::Null) => format!("{label} is required ({kind})"),
    91	            Some(value) => format!("{label} must be {kind}, not {}", describe(value)),
    92	        }));
    93	    }
    94	    Ok(())
    95	}
    96	
    97	// @ref LLP 3000.007#5.4 — nested/optional columns use the same minimum on server and device writes.
    98	fn validate_column_minimum(kind: &ColumnType, value: Option<&Value>, label: &str) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
    99	    match (kind, value) {
   100	        (ColumnType::Optional(inner), Some(value)) => validate_column_minimum(inner, Some(value), label),
   101	        (ColumnType::List { of, .. } | ColumnType::ListRange { of, .. }, Some(Value::Array(items))) => {
   102	            if let ColumnType::ListRange { min, .. } = kind { validate_list_minimum(label, items.len(), *min)?; }
   103	            for item in items { validate_column_minimum(of, Some(item), label)?; }
   104	            Ok(())
   105	        }
   106	        _ => Ok(()),
   107	    }
   108	}
   109	
   110	fn validate_list_minimum(label: &str, length: usize, min: u32) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
   111	    if length < min as usize {
   112	        return Err(RuntimeError::Input(format!("{label} needs at least {min} {}; got {length}", if min == 1 { "item" } else { "items" })));
   113	    }
   114	    Ok(())
   115	}
   116	
   117	fn describe(value: &Value) -> String {
   118	    match value {
   119	        Value::String(text) if text.chars().count() > 24 => format!("{} characters of text", text.chars().count()),
   120	        Value::String(text) => format!("the text '{text}'"),
   121	        Value::Id(id) => format!("the id '{id}'"),
   122	        Value::Int(n) => format!("the number {n}"),
   123	        Value::Bool(b) => format!("the boolean {b}"),
   124	        Value::Null => "null".into(),
   125	        Value::Bytes(b) => format!("{} bytes", b.len()),
   126	        Value::Array(items) => format!("a list of {}", items.len()),
   127	        Value::Row(_) | Value::Map(_) => "an object".into(),
   128	    }
   129	}
   130	
   131	pub fn validate_text(label: &str, text: &str, min: u32, format: Option<TextFormat>) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
   132	    let length = text.chars().count();
   133	    if length < min as usize {
   134	        return Err(RuntimeError::Input(format!("{label} needs at least {min} characters; got {length}")));
   135	    }
   136	    if let Some(format) = format
   137	        && !format.matches(text)
   138	    {
   139	        return Err(RuntimeError::Input(match format {
   140	            TextFormat::Handle => format!("{label} must be a handle: lowercase letters, digits and underscores"),
   141	            TextFormat::Url => format!("{label} must be an http or https URL"),
   142	            TextFormat::Email => format!("{label} must be an ASCII email address with one @, a dot-safe local part, and valid domain labels"),
   143	        }));
   144	    }
   145	    Ok(())
   146	}
   147	
   148	fn disallowed_control(kind: &ColumnType, value: Option<&Value>) -> Option<u32> {
   149	    match (kind, value) {
   150	        (ColumnType::Optional(inner), Some(value)) => disallowed_control(inner, Some(value)),
   151	        (ColumnType::List { of, .. } | ColumnType::ListRange { of, .. }, Some(Value::Array(values))) => values.iter().find_map(|value| disallowed_control(of, Some(value))),
   152	        (ColumnType::Text { .. }, Some(Value::String(text))) => disallowed_text_control(text),
   153	        _ => None,
   154	    }
   155	}
   156	
   157	/// Text forbids C0 controls except tab, LF and CR.
   158	pub fn disallowed_text_control(text: &str) -> Option<u32> {
   159	    text.chars().find(|c| c.is_control() && !matches!(c, '\t' | '\n' | '\r')).map(|c| c as u32)
   160	}
   161	
   162	fn fits(kind: &ColumnType, value: Option<&Value>) -> bool {
   163	    match (kind, value) {
   164	        (ColumnType::Optional(_), None | Some(Value::Null)) => true,
   165	        (ColumnType::Optional(inner), Some(value)) => fits(inner, Some(value)),
   166	        (ColumnType::List { of, max } | ColumnType::ListRange { of, max, .. }, Some(Value::Array(values))) => values.len() <= *max as usize && values.iter().all(|value| fits(of, Some(value))),
   167	        // @ref LLP 3000#7-decisions-during-execution — typed references do not imply target existence
   168	        (ColumnType::Id | ColumnType::Principal | ColumnType::Ref { .. }, Some(Value::Id(_))) => true,
   169	        (ColumnType::Text { max }, Some(Value::String(text))) => text.chars().count() <= *max as usize,
   170	        (ColumnType::Bytes { max }, Some(Value::Bytes(bytes))) => bytes.len() <= *max as usize,
   171	        (ColumnType::Image { .. } | ColumnType::Video { .. } | ColumnType::File { .. }, Some(Value::Map(fields))) => fields.get("id").is_some_and(|id| matches!(id, Value::Id(_))),
   172	        (ColumnType::Enum { variants }, Some(Value::String(value))) => variants.contains(value),
   173	        (ColumnType::Int | ColumnType::Time | ColumnType::Decimal { .. } | ColumnType::Money { .. }, Some(Value::Int(value))) => (I53_MIN..=I53_MAX).contains(value),
   174	        // @ref LLP 3000.040#14-where-the-bound-is-enforced — the slot's bound is part of its type: every stored write and argument meets it here.
   175	        (ColumnType::IntRange { min, max }, Some(Value::Int(value))) => (I53_MIN..=I53_MAX).contains(value) && (*min..=*max).contains(value),
   176	        (ColumnType::Bool, Some(Value::Bool(_))) => true,
   177	        (ColumnType::Json { max_bytes }, Some(value)) => canonical_json(value).len() <= *max_bytes as usize,
   178	        _ => false,
   179	    }
   180	}
   181	
   182	/// Validate the arguments and fill each omitted optional with `null`, so
   183	/// a caller that leaves `c` out reads page one.
   184	#[cfg(not(all(target_arch = "wasm32", feature = "web", not(feature = "sqlite"))))]
   185	pub fn validate_inputs(schema: &Schema, program: &Program, inputs: &mut BTreeMap<String, Value>) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
   186	    super::admission::check(schema, program)?;
   187	    validate_arguments(schema, program, inputs)
   188	}

# Totals: count and sum in queries, mutations, jobs and views

Count rows or add up a column with `count` and `sum` inside a query, mutation or job. A view keeps a total per group current on every write, so reading it costs one row.

```quarry
table orders:
  owner: principal
  status: text <=10
  cents: int
  at: time default now
  immutable owner, at
  read <- .owner = viewer
  insert <- .owner = viewer
  update <- deny
  delete <- deny
  online only
view spentByOwner: sum orders of .cents by .owner
  online only
view spentPerDay: sum orders of .cents by .owner, day(.at)
  online only

query myTotals(since: time):
  open = count(orders where .owner = viewer and .status = 'open')
  recent = sum(orders where .owner = viewer and .at >= since, .cents)
  return { open, recent, lifetime: spentByOwner[viewer], today: spentPerDay[viewer, day(now)], yesterday: spentPerDay[viewer, day(now) - 1] }
```

There are two spellings, and they do not mix:

- **Inside an operation** (query, mutation, job): `count(t where …)` and
  `sum(t where …, .col)`. The parentheses are required; the column is the
  second argument, written `.col`. `min` and `max` take the same arguments.
- **In a view** (declared at top level): `view name: count t by .key` or
  `view name: sum t of .col by .key`, optionally `where …` before `by`
  (`sum t of .col where … by .key`, in that order). Read
  it by its group key, `spentByOwner[viewer]`; a group with no rows reads `0`.
  Omit `by` for one global scalar, `view orderCount: count orders`, and read it
  as `orderCount`. Its authorized empty value is `0` even though zero has no
  physical aggregate row.
  A view over an `online only` table is read online; its own `online only`
  line is optional.

## In a job

A job's totals are ordinary domain reads inside its sweep; write them where a
screen can read them:

```quarry
table readings:
  owner: principal
  value: int
  at: time default now
  immutable owner, at
  read <- .owner = viewer
  insert <- .owner = viewer
  update <- deny
  delete <- deny
  online only
table sensors:
  owner: principal
  read <- .owner = viewer
  insert <- .owner = viewer
  update <- deny
  delete <- deny
  online only
table dailyStats:
  owner: principal
  day: time
  n: int
  total: int
  read <- .owner = viewer
  insert <- deny
  update <- deny
  delete <- deny
  online only

job dailyTotals(periodStart: time, periodEnd: time):
  for s in sensors by .id:
    n = count(readings where .owner = s.owner and .at >= periodStart and .at < periodEnd)
    total = sum(readings where .owner = s.owner and .at >= periodStart and .at < periodEnd, .value)
    insert dailyStats { owner: s.owner, day: periodStart, n, total }
schedule nightly every 1d at 00:05: dailyTotals
```

`periodStart` and `periodEnd` are the schedule's period, so each run totals
one day. When the job only needs a number a screen shows, a view is usually
simpler than a job: `view perDay: sum readings of .value by .owner, day(.at)`
read as `perDay[viewer, day(now)]`.

## Rows the caller partly reads

An inline total counts only what its caller may read, so `check` refuses one
whose read rule its pins do not decide (`E_QUARRY_BOUND`, or
`E_STRUCTURAL_FANOUT` in a mutation): it would be a different number for
each caller, and a capacity check would miss hidden rows. Three exact forms:
pin the deciding column (`.owner = viewer`: the caller's own total); or keep
the total in a view whose read rule names who sees it (the view discloses the
total, never the rows); or, in a mutation, `require` below a view's count.

```quarry
table staff:
  principal: principal
  role: text <=20
  unique principal
  read <- .principal = viewer
  online only
table requests:
  owner: principal
  dept: text <=20
  amount: money USD
  read <- .owner = viewer or exists staff[principal = viewer, role = 'finance']
  insert <- .owner = viewer
  online only
view requestsTotal: sum requests of .amount by .dept
  read <- exists staff[principal = viewer, role = 'finance']
view requestsByOwner: sum requests of .amount by .owner, .dept
  read <- .owner = viewer

query deptTotal(dept: text <=20):
  return requestsTotal[dept] ! else NOT_ALLOWED
query myDeptTotal(dept: text <=20):
  return requestsByOwner[viewer, dept]
```

Finance reads every department's total; each requester reads their own.
`E_QUARRY_BOUND`'s rewrite prints this view for your table, column and group.
A keyed private view stays nullable until its own read rule is proved. For
example, after `require exists staff[principal = viewer, role = 'finance']
else NOT_FINANCE`, `requestsTotal[dept] + 1` is well typed and a readable
missing group contributes zero. A hidden stored group and a hidden miss both
remain null. A following write or viewer change invalidates the proof before
another keyed read.
A view may omit `by` for a whole-table scalar: `view requestsCount: count
requests`, read as bare `requestsCount`. The scalar still obeys its view read
rule: an authorized absence is zero, while a hidden stored or absent value is
null. Keep a real group key when callers need independent totals.
A guard on the read rule's own grant admits an inline total, as it admits a
staff page (`guide indexes`): after `require exists staff[principal = viewer,
role = 'finance'] else …` (or inside `if` on it), `sum(requests, .amount)` is
every row's total, because every caller who reaches it reads every row. A guard
on any other role does not; use the view.

For extrema over a domain, first establish that exact domain is non-empty:
`n = count(orders where .owner = viewer)` followed by `require n > 0 else
EMPTY`. From there, `min`, `max` and `sum` of a required column on the identical
domain are required values. Optional projected columns remain optional. A
write, local rebinding, viewer/authority scope change, or different key/filter
invalidates the proof.

## Choosing

- A total a screen shows on every visit: a view. It is maintained on each
  write and read by key, constant work regardless of history.
- A rolling window (last 7 days): bucket the view by `day(.at)` (or
  `hour(.at)`, `bucket(.at, 15m)`) and add the buckets you need with point
  reads, `perDay[viewer, day(now)] + perDay[viewer, day(now) - 1]`.
- A one-off or filtered total (since an argument, by status): `count(...)` or
  `sum(...)` in the operation. It streams the domain under the meter: each
  row is authorized, filtered, folded and released. Its work grows with the
  matching rows, and `check --cost` says so; bound the predicate by an indexed
  time or a group key.
- `count(page)` and `sum(page, .col)` over a page you already read count only
  that page, not the population.

## Details

A meter cut never yields a partial total: the read is unavailable (the client
shows `loading`), including before `??`. On a device, an inline total needs
the device to hold its whole domain; otherwise the observation stays
`loading`, never a partial total. Sums take `int`, `decimal` or `money` columns; a total
that overflows refuses the transaction. `guide maintains` has every view form
(filters, reference paths, time buckets, offsets and sync); `guide reads`
covers totals beside pages and `exists`; `guide jobs` the sweep a job's totals
live in.

Common misspellings and what `check` says:

- `sum(orders of .cents)` (the view spelling inside an operation):
  `E_QUARRY_UNKNOWN: unknown shape of`. Write `sum(orders where …, .cents)`.
- `count orders where .owner = viewer` without parentheses:
  `E_QUARRY_UNKNOWN: unknown shape where`. Write `count(orders where …)`.
- `sum(orders where …, cents)`: `E_QUARRY_UNKNOWN: unknown name cents`. The
  column is `.cents`.

Reference-grouped views over `read through .parent` rows inherit the parent's
rule. Omit a read line that repeats `.parent.owner = viewer`; it is
`E_REDUNDANT_RULE`, and deleting the whole line preserves the inherited audience.
Maintained sums spell the value separately: `view total: sum expenses of .amount by .household`,
not `sum expenses.amount by .household`.

    56	    pub(super) fn job_query_call(&mut self, name: &str, args: &[A], site: &Site, out: &mut setup::Body) -> Option<Fallible<(Expr, Ty)>> {
    57	        if self.viewer_depth.is_none() || self.catalog.functions.contains_key(name) { return None; }

    59	        let (name, args) = if all {
    60	            match args { [A::Call { name, args, .. }] => (name.as_str(), args.as_slice()), _ => return Some(Err(diagnostic(site, "E_JOB_VIEWER_QUERY", "all takes a declared paged query call", "for row in all(unreadPreview(periodStart, periodEnd, null)):"))) }

    64	        let caller_module = std::path::Path::new(&site.file).file_stem().and_then(|s| s.to_str()).unwrap_or("schema");
    65	        let (module, decl) = self.catalog.documents.iter().find_map(|doc| {
    66	            let module = std::path::Path::new(&doc.file).file_stem()?.to_str()?;
    67	            doc.programs.iter().find(|decl| super::super::operation_name(module, &decl.name) == name || module == caller_module && decl.name == name).map(|decl| (module.to_owned(), decl.clone()))
    68	        })?;
    69	        Some((|| {
    70	            if self.viewer_depth.is_none() || decl.kind != ProgramKind::Query || decl.native || decl.held {
    71	                return Err(diagnostic(site, "E_JOB_VIEWER_QUERY", "only declared Quarry queries may be called inside a job as block", "compute with a query inside as; call mutations, actions and native operations outside it"));
    72	            }
    73	            let mut diagnostics = Vec::new();
    74	            let query = Binder::bind(self.catalog, self.schema, &module, &decl, &mut diagnostics)
    75	                .ok_or_else(|| diagnostics.into_iter().next().unwrap_or_else(|| diagnostic(site, "E_JOB_VIEWER_QUERY", "query could not be bound", "fix the declared query first")))?;
    76	            if snapback4_core::ir::read_sites(&query.program).iter().any(|read| self.schema.table(&read.table).is_some_and(|table| table.ephemeral.is_some())) {
    77	                return Err(diagnostic(site, "E_JOB_VIEWER_QUERY", "job viewer queries read durable data", "keep ephemeral reads in a client query; persist the state the job needs"));
    78	            }
    79	            if args.len() != query.args.len() { return Err(type_error(site, format!("{name} takes {} arguments", query.args.len()), "pass the same positional arguments as the declared query, including its cursor")); }
    80	            let mut fields = BTreeMap::new();
    81	            for ((name, expected), given) in query.args.iter().zip(args) {
    82	                let (value, ty) = if expected.required().is_identity() { self.expr_as_reference(given, out, expected)? } else { self.expr(given, out)? };
    83	                if !ty.assignable_to(expected) { return Err(type_error(given.site(), format!("{name} requires {}, got {}", expected.name(), ty.name()), "pass the query's declared argument type")); }
    84	                fields.insert(name.clone(), value);
    85	            }
    86	            let cursors: Vec<_> = query.args.iter().filter(|(_, ty)| ty.required() == Ty::Cursor).map(|(name, _)| name.clone()).collect();
    87	            let cursor = (cursors.len() == 1).then(|| cursors[0].clone());
    88	            let ty = if all {
    89	                let Some((item, _)) = query.result.sequence() else { return Err(diagnostic(site, "E_JOB_VIEWER_QUERY", "all needs a query returning a row list", "return the paged list from the query")); };
    90	                if cursor.is_none() || snapback4_core::engine::drain_cursor(&query.program) != cursor { return Err(diagnostic(site, "E_JOB_VIEWER_QUERY", "all needs one cursor argument that owns the returned page", "declare c: cursor ? and return a page after c; start with null")); }
    91	                Ty::List(Box::new(item), u32::MAX)
    92	            } else { Ty::Object(BTreeMap::from([("data".into(), query.result), ("next".into(), Ty::Cursor.optional())])) };
    93	            Ok((Expr::Builtin { builtin: Builtin::JobQuery { program: Box::new(query.program), cursor, all }, args: vec![Expr::Object(fields)] }, ty))
    94	        })())
    95	    }
    96	}
